# Qualified ACE behaviours

ACE behaviours qualified for the reference build under The Plan §8.1: Office Click-to-Run 16.0.20527.20196 x64,
the native raw IDispatch lane, unless a note says otherwise. A note is written as soon as the records support its
claim. Its open follow-up questions do not block it, and a later contradicting record reopens it. A difference found
on another ACE build is recorded beside the note, with both builds' records.

Each note has this form:

- **Claim**, in one or two sentences: what ACE does.
- **Conditions**: formats, routes and lanes it was asked through.
- **Records**: the supporting records, and the discriminating ones that ruled out competing explanations.
- **Open**: follow-up questions, each a candidate probe.
- **Other builds**: only when a difference has been found.

The notes below were drafted by Sonnet sub-agents from the retained records, completed with the follow-up probes of the
qualification round (`evidence/r-q-*`, 2026-11-17), and spot-checked against the records. Each section's branch status
table (Q qualified, Q+T qualified with a troublesome remainder, F follow-up needed, T troublesome, N not an ACE question)
is summarised in [qualification-report.md](qualification-report.md).

## R-ORACLE-1, R-STRING-1 and R-ACT-1

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-IDENT: the loaded ACE modules are identified by path, version and hash, stably, on the DAO and ADO lanes
- **Claim.** In every capture the module snapshots identify the loaded ACE binaries through public OS enumeration: `ACEDAO.DLL` 16.0.20527.20196
  (sha256 prefix 335ca6761d67f6e2), `ACECORE.DLL`, `ACEES.DLL`, `ACEWSTR.DLL` 16.0.20527.20196, `ACEOLEDB.DLL` 16.0.20527.20196 (71cc23e41a82141e),
  `ACEERR.DLL` 16.0.20527.20016 and `ACEINTL.DLL` 16.0.20326.20022. Nothing of ACE is loaded before activation (59 modules, 0 identified).
  DAO: creating DBEngine loads `ACEDAO.DLL` (82 modules, 14 identified). ADO: creating `ADODB.Connection` loads only `msado15.dll` (65, 1 identified), assigning the
  Provider loads nothing more, `Open` loads `ACEOLEDB.DLL`, `ACECORE.DLL`, `ACEWSTR.DLL` (150, 18), a first read loads `ACEES.DLL` (155, 22), `Close` unloads nothing,
  and creating DAO.DBEngine.120 afterwards in the same process then loads `ACEDAO.DLL` (156, 23). A connectionless `ADODB.Recordset` loads no ACE module (70 loaded, 2 identified).
- **Conditions.** Native raw IDispatch lane, .NET 11 worker, locale 7177, ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-oracle-1/create-fixture-t1`, `failed-call-t1`, `table-read-t1`; `r-q-wire/ident-ado-lazy-load/` (snapshots after create, Provider, Open, read, Close, DAO create, DAO open, DAO close);
  `r-q-wire/ado-connectionless-recordset/`; the same `modules` events in all later records. Truncation control: the `deliberate-truncation` event, `complete=False`, "buffer smaller than module list", in every record.
- **Open.** Access lane (needs the Access host: T); the owner's 20016 DAO report against the 20196 module.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: a Decimal column made by DAO `CreateField(name, 20)` has Precision 38 Scale 0 and misreads; with explicit precision every route is exact
- **Claim.** Columns made by ADO DDL as `DECIMAL(28,0)` and `DECIMAL(18,4)` store and return every tested value exactly, with all three 32-bit words, through DAO `Field.Value`, SQL `CStr`/`CDbl`,
  filters and aggregates, ADO `Value` and `GetRows`; the 29-digit maximum is refused with 3761 (DAO `0x800A0EB1`, ADO `0x80040E21`). Columns made by DAO `TableDef.CreateField` without precision report
  Precision 38 and Scale 0: SQL literal inserts succeed, DAO `Field.Value` of a non-Null value fails 3000 "Reserved error (-1038)" (`0x800A0BB8`), DAO puts fail, every ADO put fails, and ADO reads
  return the stored integer with `Lo32` moved into `Mid32` (7 reads as 30064771072) and fractions truncated at scale 0.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB and MDB alike; writes by SQL literal, DAO put and ADO put; reads by five routes; values with distinct words.
- **Records.** `r-dec-read/{accdb,mdb}-{sql-literal,dao-put,ado-put}/` (exact); `r-dec-read/{accdb,mdb}-daofield-{sql-literal,dao-put,ado-put}/` (misread); `r-fix-wire-ado/create-fdec-dao-put`, `create-fdec-sql-literal`.
  Discriminators: precision is the only difference between the two groups, and the same ADO route decodes the explicit-precision columns exactly, which rules out a capture or ADO-decode artefact.
- **Open.** Which layer moves the words (not separable through the API); a DAO route to a Decimal column with a chosen precision.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: Decimal precision 1 to 28, scale at most the precision; out-of-range values are refused, words and scale are exact
- **Claim.** ADO DDL accepts `DECIMAL(28,0)`, `(1,1)`, `(18,18)`, and refuses `(29,0)`, `(38,0)` and `(0,0)` with "Invalid precision for decimal data type" (SQLState 3700) and `(28,29)` with
  "Invalid scale" (3701). On `DECIMAL(1,0)`, `(5,2)`, `(28,28)` and `(28,0)` columns the stored value keeps its column scale (-0.5 in `(28,28)` reads scale 28, sign 0x80, exact words) and DAO
  `Value`, SQL `CStr` and ADO `Value` agree. A SQL literal beyond the column precision is refused under dbFailOnError with 3761 (`0x800A0EB1`: 10 in `(1,0)`, 1000 in `(5,2)`, 1 in `(28,28)`);
  a 29-digit literal into `(28,0)` fails `0x800A0006` Overflow. 0.005 and 0.004 into `(5,2)` and a 29th decimal place into `(28,28)` are stored as zero without error.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; ADO DDL; SQL literals under dbFailOnError; DAO and ADO reads.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/dec-ddl-precision-limits/`, `r-q-wire/dec-scale-endpoints/` (the first read-back on `(1,0)`, `(5,2)`, `(28,28)`, `(28,0)` by DAO, `CStr` and ADO).
- **Open.** Truncation or banker's rounding at a scale tie (the only tie asked is 0.005 into `(5,2)`, stored 0.00, which both rules give); MDB.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE / R-STRING-1: a leading U+FEFF in Text and Memo is not preserved; the remaining units are decoded as a compressed-Unicode stream; U+FFFE, U+FFFF and U+FFFD are kept
- **Claim.** After the value is stored and the database reopened, in both TEXT(255) and MEMO (always the same in both), a value whose first unit is U+FEFF reads back changed: exactly U+FEFF gives `""` (`Len` 0);
  U+FEFF `ab` gives `ab` (`Len` 2); U+FEFF euro gives U+00AC U+0020 (the two bytes AC 20 of U+20AC read as Latin-1; `Len` 2); U+FEFF e-acute gives U+00E9 (`Len` 1); U+FEFF U+FEFF gives U+00FF U+00FE;
  U+FEFF U+FFFE gives U+00FE U+00FF. A U+FEFF in the middle (`a` U+FEFF `b`) or at the end (`ab` U+FEFF) is kept (`Len` 3). U+FFFE alone, leading, trailing, inside and doubled, U+FFFF and U+FFFD are kept
  (`Len` and `AscW` as expected). These outcomes fit one rule: the bytes FF FE of a stored leading U+FEFF are the compression marker, and the following bytes are decoded as a compressed stream (single bytes as Latin-1,
  a 00 byte toggling to UTF-16 units). That is a hypothesis fitted to nine short rows, not a byte observation. A string of U+FEFF followed by 100 `x` is kept whole (`Len` 101): the rule does not hold for it.
  Inside the writing session, a re-fetch of the same open dynaset (`MoveFirst`, read) returned every U+FEFF value unchanged; the loss appears only after close and reopen.
- **Conditions.** Each value written by six routes and read by two: DAO dynaset put, SQL literal, SQL `ChrW(65279)` expression, QueryDef parameter (rows lone, leading, trailing, plain), ADO recordset put, ADO SQL insert;
  read by a DAO snapshot (Value, `Len`, `AscW`) and by an ADO recordset (Value, `Len`). ACCDB for all, MDB for the DAO put route (identical). A column made by ADO DDL `WITH COMPRESSION` (accepted) and one without behave the same.
- **Records.** `r-text-fffe/{accdb,mdb}-{dao-put,sql-literal}/` (U+FFFE, U+FFFF, U+FFFD, doubled and lone U+FEFF rows, read directly); `r-q-wire/feff-accdb-dao-put/`, `feff-accdb-sql-literal/`, `feff-accdb-chrw-expr/`,
  `feff-accdb-param/`, `feff-accdb-ado-put/`, `feff-accdb-ado-sql/`, `feff-mdb-dao-put/`, `feff-accdb-dao-put-refetch/` (in-session re-fetch), `feff-accdb-compression-and-properties/`.
  Discriminators: every write route and both read routes give the same result, so the change is not in the DAO string path or in one API layer; the euro and e-acute rows show that later stored bytes are re-decoded, which a
  writer that simply dropped a BOM would not do; compressed against uncompressed column shows no difference.
- **Corrected.** The earlier draft claimed only a value that is exactly U+FEFF is lost and left open whether other U+FEFF positions are affected: a leading U+FEFF is affected whatever follows (short strings), a trailing or inner one is not.
- **Open.** Why the 101-unit value survives (length, storage form or content) needs values between 3 and 101 units; whether the loss is at write or read at the byte level needs the stored bytes (W-04, T); the `UnicodeCompression`
  flag is not among the 24 DAO field properties, so the column's compression state is not verified.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: Single and Double values are preserved bit for bit through DAO put and read; SQL literals are not bit-exact
- **Claim.** DAO `Field.Value` puts of Single and Double keep -0, subnormals, ±Inf, quiet and signalling NaN and NaN payloads exactly (for example R4 `4523c17f`, R8 `230100000000f87f`) through to the DAO read and `GetRows`;
  ADO returns the same cell values. A SQL literal `-0.0` stores +0 (SQL text cannot carry negative zero, NaN or Inf).
- **Conditions.** DAO dynaset put into SINGLE and DOUBLE fields; ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-wire/put-tags-single/`, `put-tags-double/`, `read-dao/`, `ado-getrows-tags/`; the SQL contrast `r-wire/create-sql/`, `read-sql/`.
- **Open.** MDB repeat; whether the SQL parser folds `-0.0` as the negation of an integer zero is not separated from a storage effect.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE / R-STRING-1: an odd-byte BSTR is refused for Text and Memo puts, truncated for parameters, and stored byte-exact in Binary and OLE
- **Claim.** A BSTR whose byte count is odd put into a Text field through DAO fails `0x80020009` with EXCEPINFO `0x800A0FCB` "Number of bytes of a UNICODE string is not even"; into a Memo, EXCEPINFO `0x800A0E76`;
  through ADO `0x80040E21`. A QueryDef Text or Memo parameter silently truncates the BSTR to an even length (1 byte gives `""`, 3 bytes give "A"). Into BINARY and LONGBINARY fields through `Field.Value` the BSTR payload
  is stored as its bytes with its exact length: 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5-byte BSTRs store 1, 2, 3, 4 and 5 bytes (`41`, `4100`, `410042`, `41004200`, `4100420043`), BINARY(16) pads with zero bytes to 16, and the DAO and ADO reads agree.
  A BSTR text (`"AB"`) stores its UTF-16 bytes (`41004200`; U+0141 U+0242 stores `41014202`). NUL and lone surrogates are stored in Text. After a failed put, `Update` still succeeds and leaves a Null cell (a zero-length string for Memo).
- **Corrected.** The earlier draft said an odd 3-byte BSTR stores 4 bytes in Binary and OLE; that figure came from the `AppendChunk` route (`r-fix-wire-ado/chunk-ole-append-fieldsize`, digest), not the `Field.Value` put, which is byte-exact.
- **Conditions.** Raw BSTR with exact bytes; DAO put, ADO put, QueryDef parameter, AppendChunk; ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-wire/put-tags-text/`, `put-tags-memo/` (read directly: 1-byte BSTR refused with the two scodes), `create-dao/`, `put-tags-binary/`, `put-tags-ole/`, `param-select-text/`, `chunk-memo-text-append/`;
  `r-fix-wire-ado/ado-write-values-text-binary`, `chunk-ole-append-fieldsize`, `chunk-memo-text-append-fieldsize`; `r-q-wire/str-odd-bstr-into-binary/` (seven values, DAO and ADO reads).
- **Open.** Text coercion of an odd BSTR inside expressions; the Memo parameter order dependence recorded as a defect.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: integer admission through SQL literals: Byte wraps modulo 256, Integer and Long refuse out-of-range, fractions round half to even
- **Claim.** `INSERT` of a literal under dbFailOnError: into BYTE, 256, 257, 511, 512, -1 and -2 store 0, 1, 255, 0, 255 and 254 (wrapped modulo 256, no error); 255.4 stores 255 and 255.5 stores 0. Into SHORT, 32768, 32769, 65535,
  65536, -32769 and -65536 fail `0x800A0006` Overflow and the row is not inserted; 32767 and -32768 store. Into LONG, 2147483648, 4294967295, 4294967296 and -2147483649 fail `0x800A0D88` "Data type mismatch"; 2147483647 and
  -2147483648 store. Fractions round half to even in all three: 0.5 stores 0, 1.5 stores 2, 2.5 stores 2. (Without dbFailOnError the earlier `r-wire/create-sql` shows Null for out-of-range SHORT and LONG literals: the refusal is
  turned into a Null by the non-FailOnError path, which these records do not re-ask.)
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; DAO `Execute` with dbFailOnError; one literal per statement.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/int-sql-literal-byte/`, `int-sql-literal-integer/`, `int-sql-literal-long/`; `r-wire/create-sql/`, `read-sql/`.
- **Open.** Single, Currency and Date literals; the non-FailOnError Null path per type; the ADO command-parameter route (`r-fix-wire-ado/ado-command-parameters-fresh` refuses range with `0x8002000A`).

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: OpenDatabase optional arguments, with the name given
- **Claim.** With a real path, `OpenDatabase` opens with `Errors.Count` 0 for: name only; `Options` or `ReadOnly` Missing (also both, and with `Connect` Missing); `Connect` Missing, Empty, Null or `""`; `ReadOnly` Empty or Null with `Options`
  Missing; all three Empty. `Updatable` is -1 for all of them, so `ReadOnly` is ignored when `Options` is Missing. `(False, True)` and `(I2 0, I2 -1)` give `Updatable` 0. `Options` as Null, with or without `ReadOnly` True, or as
  BSTR "x" fails `0x80020009`, EXCEPINFO `0x800A0D5D` ("Data type conversion error", source `DAO.Workspace`), `DBEngine.Errors` 1 × 3421, with no IErrorInfo.
- **Conditions.** One-attempt variants; the name is never Empty, Null or Missing (those open a modal ODBC dialog).
- **Records.** `r-fix-wire-ado/opt-opendatabase-*` (15 records), `r-q-wire/opt-opendatabase-options-null-alone/` (the isolated Null in the Options position: `(path, Null)` and `(path, Null, True)` both 3421; control `(path, False, True)` opens).
- **Open.** The Empty, Null and Missing name variants are interactive (ODBC dialog) and are not to be asked outside a dialog-handling route (T).

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-WIRE: Rnd is deterministic after a negative seed, reproducible across queries and processes, and evaluated once per query
- **Claim.** `Rnd(-1)` returns 0.2240070104598999 and the following `Rnd(1)` calls 0.035845816135406494, 0.08635234832763672, and `Rnd()` then 0.16426390409469604; `Rnd(0)` returns the last value (0.16426390409469604); `Rnd(-5)` then `Rnd(1)`
  returns 0.8383256793022156 then 0.2874332666397095. The same sequence comes back in a second query of the same process and equals the value seen in an earlier independent process (`r-wire/expr-clock-random`: 0.2240070104598999).
  With constant arguments every row of a query gets the same value (the expression is evaluated once per query, not per row).
- **Conditions.** Snapshots of a 3-row table; constant arguments; ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/rnd-seeded-repeat/` (five queries); `r-wire/expr-clock-random/`.
- **Open.** `Now()` and `Timer()` are observed once (they differ between queries 1.1 s apart) and not repeated; per-row Rnd with a column argument.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-OBS: DAO `Errors` holds the latest failure and is never cleared by a success; ADO `Errors` is cleared by the next successful operation; the channels depend on the call
- **Claim.** DAO: after a failure, `DBEngine.Errors` has Count 1 holding that failure (3376, then 3011, then 3078, then 3192, then 3376, each failure replacing the previous); a following successful `OpenRecordset` or
  `Execute` leaves the old entry in place (3078 stays through a successful OpenRecordset and a successful UPDATE; a marker 3376 stays through a successful `OpenDatabase`). ADO: `Connection.Errors` holds 1 after a failed `Open` (SQLState 3044) and
  after a failed `Execute` (SQLState 3000), and is 0 after a successful `Open` and after every successful `Execute`. Channels in the raw lane: DAO engine failures (missing table 3011, `DROP TABLE` of a missing table, bad SQL, a bad
  `Options` type, any member call on a retained object after Close except the `Field.Value` get) carry an EXCEPINFO with source `DAO.<class>` and no IErrorInfo (`GetErrorInfo` S_FALSE); failures of the `Field.Value` get carry EXCEPINFO and an
  IErrorInfo with a zero GUID, whether the error is an expression error (`AscW("")`, `0x800A0005`, `r-text-fffe`) or 3420 on a retained Field (`retain-recordset-field-after-db-close`: `Value` has it, `Name` and the recordset calls do not);
  the same error code `0x800A0005` raised at `OpenRecordset` (`Sqr(-1)` in the projection) carries no IErrorInfo. ADO failures carry both, with the provider GUID `{0C733A..}`.
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch; worker captures EXCEPINFO and `GetErrorInfo` straight after each failing call; a marker failure precedes each DAO step.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/obs-errors-replace-persist/`, `obs-ado-errors-clear/`, `opt-opendatabase-options-null-alone/` (persistence across a successful open), `obs-engine-versus-expression-errors/` (the engine failures and the 0x800A0005 at open);
  `r-text-fffe/*` (Field.Value expression errors with IErrorInfo); `r-str-ado-act/str-schema-change`, `r-fix-wire-ado/str-schema-change-isolated` (old 3211 persists after a successful ALTER).
- **Open.** The variant "diagnostic getter first against interfering getter or cleanup" is not asked (the worker reads the channels at fixed points; T); VBA, managed and vtable lanes (T). The IErrorInfo rule (a `Field.Value` get has one,
  other DAO calls do not) rests on records from different probes, because the probe meant to read `Field.Value` expression errors beside engine errors stopped at `OpenRecordset` (see the status file).

### R-STRING-1 STR-READ: a DAO Text or Memo value reads identically by ordinal, by name, from a retained Field, across projections and after revisits
- **Claim.** For the eleven-row fixture (empty string, Null, embedded NUL, a NUL alone, 4,000-unit Memos with NUL, a 70,000-unit Memo, a supplementary character, mixed Latin-1 and wide text) the VT tag, BSTR byte length and text of each
  value are identical by ordinal and by name, between a retained Field object and a fresh one, with one or nine projected columns, with a duplicated column, on repeat reads and after re-finding a row; the ADO read of the same rows
  returns the same tags and `ActualSize`. Embedded NUL is kept (`Len` counts it, `InStr(T, Chr(0))` finds it). `""` and Null stay distinct in DAO and ADO `Value`. After EOF a retained Field fails `0x800A0BCD`; after `Recordset.Close` it fails `0x800A0D5C`.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB for all probes; MDB for the ordinal pass (identical to ACCDB); snapshot recordsets; DAO-written `S` and ADO-written compressed `C` tables.
- **Records.** `r-str-ado-act/str-ordinal-accdb/`, `str-ordinal-mdb/`, `str-named-retained/`, `str-projection/`, `str-revisit/`, `str-after-eof-close/`, `str-ado-read/`.
- **Open.** MDB for the named, projection and revisit probes; native compression forms and page and cache effects (byte-level, W-04); no allocation or timing claim.

### R-STRING-1 STR-LV: `FieldSize` of a Memo is in bytes, `GetChunk` offsets and sizes are in characters, on every recordset type
- **Claim.** For Memo values `abcde`, 3,000 `q`, `a` NUL `b` and euro a-umlaut, a property-get `FieldSize` returns 10, 6000, 6 and 4 (twice the character count) and `GetChunk(0,4)` and `GetChunk(1,4)` return the expected
  four-character slices (`abcd`, `bcde`; `a` NUL `b`, NUL `b`), identically through a snapshot, a dynaset, a table-type recordset and a forward-only recordset. A Text field refuses both (`0x800A0CBB`, `r-fix-wire-ado/str-memo-fieldsize`).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; property get of `FieldSize` (the method-call form is refused `0x800A0CB3`).
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/str-memo-fieldsize-by-recordset-type/`; `r-fix-wire-ado/str-memo-fieldsize/`, `r-str-ado-act/str-memo-chunks/`.
- **Open.** Values near 8,000 and 1 GB; the native storage forms behind them (W-04).

### R-STRING-1 STR-EDIT: NUL in Text and Memo survives Update and Requery() in MDB and ACCDB
- **Claim.** On a dynaset, T = `x` NUL `y` NUL and M = 6000 `e` plus NUL, put, `Update`, `Requery()` with no argument and `MoveFirst`, read back unchanged; a fresh snapshot gives `Len(T)` 4, `Len(M)` 6001, `InStr(T, Chr(0))` 2.
  A 256-unit T assignment fails `0x800A0C5B`; `CancelUpdate` after Update fails `0x800A0BCC` (ACCDB).
- **Conditions.** MDB (new) and ACCDB (earlier); dynaset; no-argument Requery only.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/str-edit-requery-mdb/`; `r-str-ado-act/str-edit-requery/`.
- **Open.** Edit of an existing long Memo through different storage forms (W-04).

### R-STRING-1 STR-SCHEMA: ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN under an open dynaset fails 3211; closed, it succeeds, and held objects refresh only on request
- **Claim.** `ALTER TABLE S ADD COLUMN X` while a dynaset on `S` is open fails `0x800A0C8B` with `Errors(0)` 3211 "could not lock table"; with nothing open it succeeds, and `DBEngine.Errors` still reports the old 3211 afterwards. A retained Field keeps
  reading after the failed ALTER, and after a successful ALTER `TableDefs Refresh` shows the new Field. Through ADO a keyset recordset blocks the ALTER (3211) while a client-cursor recordset does not, and the client recordset's `Fields.Count` stays 5 until `Requery` (6).
- **Conditions.** DAO dynaset and ADO keyset or client static, one element varied (open, closed, keyset only, client only), ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-fix-wire-ado/str-schema-change-isolated/`, `fields-refresh-ddl-closed/`, `fields-refresh-ddl-keyset-only/`, `fields-refresh-ddl-client-only/`; `r-str-ado-act/str-schema-change/`; the persistence of the old 3211 is also `r-q-wire/obs-errors-replace-persist/`.
- **Open.** MDB; other open recordset types.

### R-ORACLE-1 OR-REENTRY (ACE fault): `Recordset.Requery(QueryDef)` kills the process
- **Claim.** `Recordset.Requery` with a QueryDef argument on a dynaset opened from a saved parameter query ends the process with access violation `0xC0000005` while `rs.Requery` is the call in flight, whether the QueryDef is the recordset's own, a
  second instance of the same saved query, or after a parameter change. `Requery()` without argument returns normally and re-reads (one row), and so do table, dynaset, snapshot and forward-only recordsets. Through the DAO PIA (vtable) the same call
  returns `RPC_E_SERVERFAULT`, which places the fault in ACEDAO, not in the worker's argument handling (informal, not retained).
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch; dynaset; saved parameter query `QParam`; ACCDB.
- **Records.** `r-crash-requery/requery-same-qd/`, `requery-other-qd/`, `param-then-requery-qd/` (Uncertain, exit -1073741819, last native call `rs.Requery`) against `open-read/` and `requery-plain/`; independent sightings
  `r-cursor-1b/requery-parameter-query/`, `r-dao-1-err-1/query-requery/`, the original `r-cat-1/parameter-values/`, the repairs `r-fix-expr-sql/*-requery-no-argument/`; no-argument forms `r-cursor-1b/requery-types-state/`.
- **Open.** Other recordset types with an argument, and non-QueryDef arguments: deliberately not asked (crash hazard); retain the PIA/ADO observation formally (task `9fkt`).

### R-ACT-1 ACT-THREAD SAME-STA and MTA-CREATE: DAO objects are direct on an STA and proxies from an MTA; ADO objects are not proxies from an MTA; calls and errors are alike
- **Claim.** `DAO.DBEngine.120` (registered Apartment) and its Database, created on the main STA or on a second STA thread, answer `E_NOINTERFACE` for IClientSecurity, IMultiQI and IMarshal: direct objects. Created from a new MTA thread
  (`CoGetApartmentType` type 1; `CoInitializeEx` S_FALSE), they answer all three: a COM proxy. `Version`, `OpenDatabase`, a read and a failing `OpenRecordset` return the same values and EXCEPINFO (`0x800A0BC3`, source `DAO.Database`) in all three apartments.
  ADO is different: `ADODB.Connection`, `Recordset` (registered Both), `Command` (Both) and `Stream` (Apartment) answer IMarshal on the main STA and on an MTA thread alike, and none answers IClientSecurity or IMultiQI from either: no proxy,
  even for the Apartment-registered classes. A Connection used from an MTA thread opens, reads (`COUNT(*)` 3, `MAX(ID)` 3) and fails a bad statement exactly as on the STA (0x80040E14, IErrorInfo with the provider GUID, `Errors` 1).
  IStdMarshalInfo and IAgileObject are answered by none.
- **Conditions.** Thread apartment from `CoGetApartmentType` (type 0 STA, type 1 MTA); no message pump on the second STA; no concurrent callers.
- **Records.** `r-act-1-thread/dao-main-sta/`, `dao-second-sta/`, `dao-new-mta/`, `ado-*`; `r-q-wire/thread-ado-recordset-{main-sta,new-mta}/`, `thread-ado-command-{main-sta,new-mta}/`, `thread-ado-stream-{main-sta,new-mta}/`,
  `thread-ado-data-read-{main-sta,new-mta}/` (QueryInterface results in the `interfaces` events of the JSON); `r-q-wire/thread-engine-identity-*` (DAO from MTA and second STA again).
- **Open.** What makes ADO answer IMarshal everywhere (consistent with a free-threaded marshaler, not shown: needs `IMarshal` calls the harness lacks, T); server-entry thread; concurrent callers.

### R-ACT-1 ACT-THREAD ENGINE-IDENTITY: a DBEngine created in another apartment is a separate engine state; within one apartment it is the same
- **Claim.** On one STA two creations of `DAO.DBEngine.120` return one object and share Workspaces (`r-str-ado-act/act-two-engines`). A second engine created on a new MTA thread or on a second STA thread, while the main-STA engine exists, has its own
  Workspaces and Errors: a Workspace appended there (Count 1 to 2) is not seen by the main engine (Count 1, only `#Default Workspace#`), and a failed `OpenDatabase` of an absent path there (3044, `Errors` 1) leaves the main engine's `Errors` at 0.
  With the order reversed (MTA first, main STA after) the main engine again sees Count 1 and `Errors` 0. The MTA engine is a proxy (IClientSecurity answered); the second-STA engine is direct.
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch; the thread's objects are released when its block ends; ACCDB not involved.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/thread-engine-identity-main-then-mta/`, `main-then-sta2/`, `mta-then-main/`; `r-str-ado-act/act-two-engines/`.
- **Open.** Whether `Errors` is per engine instance or per thread (indistinguishable here); whether a transaction or lock state is shared (LOCAL-CONFLICT needs concurrent callers, T).

### R-ACT-1 ACT-RETAIN: after `Database.Close` or `Workspace.Close` every held child is invalid (3420); the Error objects survive
- **Claim.** With a snapshot and a dynaset still open, `Database.Close` succeeds; afterwards every member call on the held Recordsets (`EOF`, `Name`, `MoveNext`, `Close`), their Fields collections and Field (`Name`, `Value`, `Count`) fails
  `0x800A0D5C` (`Errors(0)` 3420 "Object invalid or no longer set", source `DAO.Recordset` or `DAO.Field`). A held TableDef, its Fields and Indexes, an Index, the TableDefs collection, and a saved QueryDef (`Name`, `SQL`, `Type`, `OpenRecordset`) fail the same way.
  `Workspace.Close` with a Database and a recordset open invalidates the Workspace itself (`Name`), the Database and the recordset (3420 from `DAO.Database`). A held Errors collection and its Error object stay readable (`Number` 3011, `Description`)
  after `Database.Close` and after the DBEngine reference is released, and a DBEngine created again still reports `Errors` Count 1. ADO: after `Recordset.Close` a held Field fails (`Name`, `Value`), the held Fields collection still counts 2, `State` 0;
  after `Connection.Close` the Fields collection counts 0 and `State` is 0.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB scratch databases; raw IDispatch; one parent closed per probe.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/retain-recordset-field-after-db-close/`, `retain-tabledef-after-db-close/`, `retain-querydef-after-db-close/`, `retain-database-after-workspace-close/`, `retain-errors-after-engine-release/`,
  `retain-ado-field-after-recordset-and-connection-close/`; `r-str-ado-act/str-after-eof-close/`, `ado-session`.
- **Open.** Enumerator objects, type-info objects, callback thunks, a retained factory and server locks (need a native caller, T).

### R-ACT-1 ACT-ADO-SCOPE (ACE side): a fresh ADO Connection is MSDASQL; reading `Properties` pins the provider; a connectionless Recordset needs no ACE
- **Claim.** A new `ADODB.Connection` reports `Provider` "MSDASQL". Assigning the ACE provider succeeds when it is the first member used (after nothing, a `Provider` read, `State`, `ConnectionString` or `Mode`), and fails `0x800A0C94` once
  `Properties.Count` was read (14 properties for MSDASQL), leaving `Provider` "MSDASQL.1". With the ACE provider first, `Properties` has 31 entries closed and 101 open. Opens with a non-ACE name, or with only a Data Source while `Provider` still
  reads MSDASQL, go to the ODBC driver manager and fail IM002. A connectionless `ADODB.Recordset` (`Fields.Append` of an adInteger and an adVarWChar, `Open` without a connection, `AddNew`, `Update`, `MoveFirst`) works, reads 7 and "x" back,
  has a null `ActiveConnection`, and loads no ACE module.
- **Conditions.** Raw IDispatch, one element varied per probe (the first member touched), ACE provider 12.0.
- **Records.** `r-fix-wire-ado/ado-provider-pin-isolated/`, `ado-open-provider-property-ace-first/`; `r-str-ado-act/ado-provider-non-ace/`, `ado-persist/`; `r-q-wire/ado-connectionless-recordset/`.
- **Open.** Effective omitted/default provider with a connection string only; the CalcDb routing and the host decision are not ACE questions. The non-ACE records pass through ODBC; no new probe uses it.

### R-ACT-1 ACT-NAME: `DAO.DBEngine.120` and the version-independent ADODB ProgIDs resolve; the version-dependent `ADODB.*.6.1` ProgIDs do not
- **Claim.** `CLSIDFromProgID` resolves `DAO.DBEngine.120` ({CD7791B9-...}, ACEDAO.DLL, Apartment), `ADODB.Connection` ({00000514-...}, Apartment), `ADODB.Recordset` ({00000535-...}, Both), `ADODB.Command` ({00000507-...}, Both) and `ADODB.Stream`
  ({00000566-...}, Apartment), all in `msado15.dll` for ADO. `ADODB.Connection.6.1`, `ADODB.Recordset.6.1`, `ADODB.Command.6.1` and `ADODB.Stream.6.1` fail with `0x800401F3` (invalid class string): those names are not registered on this installation.
- **Conditions.** Raw lookup of ProgID through `CLSIDFromProgID` and `CoCreateInstance` in-process, x64.
- **Records.** `r-q-wire/name-ado-versioned-progids/` (eight names, four fail); `r-str-ado-act/act-dao120/`, `act-ado-classes/`.
- **Open.** Compatibility aliases and missing-name behaviour are CalcDb-side or isolated-environment questions (ACT-MISSING, T).

## R-FMT-1, R-FMT-2, R-PRESERVE-1 and R-SORT-3

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-CREATE: CreateDatabase's version option selects the format, and only 64, 128 and 256 are accepted

- **Claim**: `DBEngine.CreateDatabase(path, ";LANGID=0x0409;CP=1252;COUNTRY=0", option)` with option 64 gives a Jet 4 MDB (`db.Version` "4.0"), 128 an ACCDB ("12.0"), 256 an ACCDB reporting "14.0"; 32 is refused with 0x800A0C62 ("Could not find installable ISAM"). The locale string needs its leading `;`: `""` gives 3001 (0x800A0BB9), a string without the leading `;` gives 0x800A0C62, `LANGID=0x0000` gives 0x800A0C9C. `;LANGID=0x0409` alone and with `;CP=1252` are accepted. A bare file is 172032 B (ACCDB) or 77824 B (MDB); a table with a Long key adds 12288 B in both.
- **Conditions**: native DAO, `eng.CreateDatabase` only; each option or spelling is its own call, locale and path fixed.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/create-accdb-empty/`, `create-mdb-empty/` (and the 50 other creations, same sizes); `evidence/r-sort-3/create-version-32/`, `create-version-256/` (attempt `a2`, "14.0"), `create-locale-spellings/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v256-corpus-accdb/` (second, independent "14.0" creation, re-read), `v128-corpus-accdb/` ("12.0"), `v64-corpus-mdb/` ("4.0"). Discriminators: option 64 versus 128 versus 256 with the same locale; 32 versus the others; spelling variants with the option fixed.
- **Open**: options 1, 8 and 16 (older Jet) were never asked.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-REOPEN and R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-NOOP: opening, enumerating, reading, edit-and-cancel and closing leave the file bytes unchanged

- **Claim**: a read-only open (`OpenDatabase(path, False, True)`) with metadata enumeration and reads of user tables does not change one byte: the SHA-256 is the same at creation, before the open and after the close, for 52 fixtures. A read/write open does the same, one element at a time: open and close; the same again; open, enumerate TableDefs, fields and QueryDefs; open, read all rows through a dynaset; open, open the table, MoveFirst, Edit and CancelUpdate. While open, a 64-byte lock file exists (`.laccdb` or `.ldb`) and it is gone after close.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, copies of one fixture each (30 rows, an index, a Memo, a saved query), whole-file hash read after Close with the DBEngine alive, each step its own open and close.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-*/` (52 records, hashes equal; re-read for the empty, counter, long and wide fixtures); `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/noop-rw-accdb/` and `noop-rw-mdb/` (re-read: `files[after-...]` hash and size equal at all five steps and at the repeat, ACCDB 200704 B and MDB 106496 B); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-noop/`. Discriminators: read-only versus read/write, repeated, with enumeration, with reads, with an Edit cancelled.
- **Open**: an Edit that is not cancelled but updates with the same value changes the file (the `fx-same-value-update` result in r-fmt-2b); lock-file contents are R-LOCK-1/2.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-SYSTEM-TABLES: with the default Admin user, every system table refuses DAO index and record access

- **Claim**: the TableDefs of a new file list the system tables (ACCDB: MSysACEs, MSysComplexColumns, MSysObjects, MSysQueries, MSysRelationships; MDB the same without MSysComplexColumns), each with `Fields.Count` 0. `Indexes.Count` on any of them fails with 3110 (0x800A0C26), `OpenRecordset` on any of them and SQL `SELECT` of MSysObjects, MSysRelationships or MSysQueries with 3112 (0x800A0C28), and the `Connection` database property's `Value` with 3251 (0x800A0CB3). User tables and their data are unaffected.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, the default Admin user, no workgroup, DAO enumeration of TableDefs and Databases properties.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-empty/` (11 failures per ACCDB read, 9 per MDB read, same codes in all 52 reads); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-noop/` and the other `pres-*` records (SQL refusals 3112). Discriminator: user tables enumerated in the same reads succeed.
- **Open**: whether another user or workgroup changes this (out of scope). Catalog-row comparison needs the W-04 byte route.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-FAMILIES: DAO reports each scalar family with a fixed Type, Size and Attributes and reads the extremes back exactly

- **Claim**: Byte (2, 1), Integer (3, 2), Long (4, 4), Single (6, 4), Double (7, 8), Currency (5, 8), Date/Time (8, 8), Text (10, size as declared), Memo (12), Yes/No (1, 1), Binary (9, size as declared), Long Binary (11), GUID (15, 16) have the same Type, Size and Attributes in ACCDB and MDB. Extremes read back exactly as VT_UI1, I2, I4, R4, R8, CY, DATE; -0 reads back as -0 for Single and Double; a Null reads VT_NULL except Yes/No. An empty byte array stored in Binary or Long Binary reads Null. A GUID reads as the BSTR `{guid {00112233-4455-6677-8899-AABBCCDDEEFF}}` (re-read: ACE's text). AutoNumber has Attributes 17, its primary key reads Primary and Unique, a descending index field reads Attributes 1.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, one family per fixture, written through DAO, read through DAO after reopen; no Decimal (see NUMERIC).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-byte/` ... `read-*-guid/` (13 families), `read-*-counter/`, `read-*-index/` and the matching `create-*` records; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/binary-bytes-accdb/` (empty arrays read Null, re-read). Discriminators: one family per fixture; the two formats side by side.
- **Open**: none that block.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-DECIMAL: Decimal columns come only from ADO DDL (or CreateField), never from DAO DDL

- **Claim**: DAO `Execute` of `CREATE TABLE ... V DECIMAL(18,4)` and of bare `V DECIMAL` is refused in ACCDB and MDB with 0x800A0CDC (3292). The same column made with ADO DDL (Provider Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0) is accepted in both formats and DAO reports Type 20, Size 16, Attributes 1, and Precision and Scale as declared; DAO reads the values as VT_DECIMAL.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB; DAO DDL, then ADO DDL on the closed file; reads through DAO.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-ddl-limits/accdb-decimal/`, `accdb-decimal-plain/`, `mdb-decimal/`, `mdb-decimal-plain/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt1-accdb-decimal/`, `fmt1-mdb-decimal/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/decimal-grid-accdb/`, `-mdb/` (re-read: Precision and Scale properties read back for (1,0), (2,1), (5,2), (18,4), (28,0), (28,28)). Discriminators: DDL route varied with format and values fixed.
- **Open**: DAO's own `CreateField(name, 20)` gives Precision 38 and an unreadable field (R-DEC-READ).

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-GATED: BIGINT and DATETIME2 exist in ACCDB and are refused in the Jet 4 MDB

- **Claim**: `ALTER TABLE T ADD COLUMN V BIGINT` and `V DATETIME2` are accepted by an ACCDB (BIGINT: Type 16, Size 8, Attributes 2, rows read VT_I8; DATETIME2: Type 26, Size 42, Attributes 1, read as a BSTR with seven fractional digits) and refused by an MDB with 0x800A0BB9 (3001). After the refusal the column does not exist.
- **Conditions**: DAO `Execute` with dbFailOnError, each statement alone.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/create-accdb-bigint/`, `create-accdb-datetime2/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt1-mdb-bigint/`, `fmt1-mdb-datetime2/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/scalar-bounds-accdb/` (BigInt) and `scalar-bounds-mdb/` (no Fbig column), `dt2-ticks-accdb/`. Discriminator: the same statement in two formats.
- **Open**: none that block.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-COLUMN-ORDER and R-FMT-2 Historical row coordinates (public side): column order, rename, drop and append are visible only through OrdinalPosition, and every old value stays with its column across the 8-bit boundaries

- **Claim**: setting `Field.OrdinalPosition` changes the listed order and the order of `SELECT *` without moving any value. After DROP COLUMN of a middle and a final column, old rows read with their remaining columns intact; after ADD COLUMN of a Long, a Text and a Yes/No, old rows read Null in the Long and Text columns and False in the Yes/No column; a renamed column keeps its values. The same holds at 7, 8, 9, 15, 16 and 17 columns: a row with every column set and one row per column with only that column Null read back exactly, and so do those rows after adding a Yes/No, a Long, a Text, dropping column C3, setting the Yes/No True and adding a second Yes/No to the 8-column table and adding a Long and a Yes/No to the 16-column table (the second added Yes/No reads False for old rows).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB (the mask probes' values are identical in both), DDL and updates through DAO, key plus Long columns.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-ordinal/`, `read-*-renamed-dropped/`; `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/alt-drop-ladder/`, `alt-append-ladder/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/mask-boundary-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: every Null pattern at 7 to 17 columns and every ALTER step; the two files' value lines are identical). Discriminators: each ALTER its own step; Null in each position separately.
- **Open**: physical column numbers, positions and the mask bytes are W-04 work (T).

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-WIDE: a table holds at most 255 fields and a record at most about 4000 bytes

- **Claim**: a table of 254 or 255 Long columns in total (key included) is created, takes a row and reads it back; a 256-column table is refused with error 3190 ("Too many fields defined"), and later statements on it find no table. A 255-column Yes/No table (key plus 254) reads False everywhere and keeps a single True set in column 100. Text(255) tables with 4 columns take a row of 255-character values; with 8 and 16 columns the table is created but the INSERT is refused with error 3047 ("Record is too large").
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, SQL DDL and INSERT with dbFailOnError and a marked Errors read after each.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/wide-accdb/` (re-read: Fields.Count 254, 255 and 255 for the three Long tables and the Yes/No table; errors 3190 and 3047), `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-wide/` (120 columns). Discriminators: column count varied 254, 255, 256 with type fixed; Text column count varied 4, 8, 16.
- **Open**: where between 4 and 8 columns of 255 characters the 3047 boundary lies (the 4000-byte rule is a guess); TableDef continuation bytes are W-04 work (T).

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-CALCULATED (part): calculated columns through DAO are admitted for Yes/No, Byte, Integer, Long, Currency, Single, Double, Memo and Decimal; two function calls are refused

- **Claim**: a TableDef made with `CreateTableDef`, whose field has `Expression` put before it is appended, is created for result types Yes/No (`[A] > 3`), Byte (`[A]`), Integer (`[A] * 2`), Long (`[A] * 100000`), Currency (`[A] * 1.5`), Single and Double (`[A] / 3`), Memo (`String(300, 'm')`) and Decimal (`[A] / 3`). Each reads Type = the requested DAO type, Attributes 2, an `Expression` property equal to the text, and a `ResultType` property (1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 7, 12 for the first eight; 16 for Decimal); the value is computed on insert and is Null when the input is Null; the Decimal result reads as VT_DECIMAL with scale 14 (1.66666666666667). `CStr([A]) & '-x'`, `[B] & '-' & CStr([A])` and `DateAdd('d', [A], #1/1/2000#)` are refused at `TableDefs.Append` with error 3859 ("cannot be used in a calculated column").
- **Conditions**: ACCDB only (calculated columns), each result type in its own table, each step's Errors read after a marker.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/calc-types-accdb/` (re-read: values and properties of the nine admitted types; 3859 for Date and Text), `calc-dependency-accdb/` (3859 on the first step); `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-accdb-calculated/`.
- **Open**: Text and Date results with an admitted expression, recomputation when an input changes or becomes Null, writing the calculated column, dropping or renaming an input: the dependency probe's own expression was refused, so its table was never created and none of those steps ran (a probe defect). Wrapper bytes are T.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-INDEX-ENUM: TableDef.Indexes enumerates in name order, case-insensitively, not in creation order

- **Claim**: after creating `Z_ix`, `M_ix`, `a_ix`, `B_ix`, `_first` and `0_ix` in that order on a table whose key index is `PK_T`, the list reads `_first`, `0_ix`, `a_ix`, `B_ix`, `M_ix`, `PK_T`, `Z_ix` (underscore before digit before letters, upper and lower case together), after every creation and after a read-only reopen.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, SQL CREATE INDEX, TableDefs and Indexes refreshed before each read.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/index-enum-order-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-index/`, `evidence/r-sort-3b/image-build-order-a/`. Discriminator: creation order differing from name order.
- **Open**: names with other punctuation or non-ASCII letters.

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-COLLATING and R-SORT-3 Descriptor dispatch (locale): the LANGID of CreateDatabase selects the collating order, and locale-tailored orders differ in listed ways

- **Claim**: `Database.CollatingOrder` equals the LANGID requested at creation: 0x0409 gives 1033, 0x0809 2057, 0x0407 1031, 0x041D 1053, 0x0405 1029, 0x0411 1041, 0x041F 1055, 0x040A 1034, 0x0419 1049, 0x0408 1032, 0x0406 1030, and a table's Text field reports the same value; it is unchanged by a read-only reopen and kept by CompactDatabase between ACCDB and MDB. On the corpora tried, General, UK, German (0x0407), Russian (0x0419) and Greek (0x0408) give identical equality classes and order, including a corpus with Cyrillic letters (ё, я, Я), Greek letters (final sigma, accented alpha) and German umlaut and sharp-s forms; Danish (0x0406) puts ä, ø, ö, å and "aa" after z; Swedish puts å, ä, ö, ø after z and ü after ue; Czech puts "ch" between è and i; Spanish-traditional puts "cz" before "ch"; Japanese orders Han 一 < 漢 < 国 < 二; Turkish sorts dotless i before {i, İ} and drops the İ row from a unique index.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB for all locales, MDB for general, German and Russian; reads by SQL ORDER BY, GROUP BY, index walk and unique survivors; each locale its own file.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt1-collating-contrast/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/locale-*/`, `lg-*`, `lu-*`; `evidence/r-sort-3/compact-*/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/locale-extra-accdb/` (re-read: CollatingOrder 1033, 1049, 1032, 1030, 1031; Russian, Greek and German walks equal General's, Danish differs). Discriminators: locale varied with corpus, version and path fixed; czech, swedish and japanese found independently by compaction and by creation.
- **Open**: a German phonebook order, Windows regional settings and registry sort options (not permitted); General97 and General - Legacy (no selector; T).

### R-FMT-1 FMT1-PROPERTIES: a TableDef Description and a user Database property can be appended and read back

- **Claim**: appending a TableDef property `Description` (Text) returns success and it reads back with Type 10 and its value; a user Database property (`CalcDbProbe`, Text) reads back in the same session and after a read-only reopen, in ACCDB and MDB.
- **Conditions**: DAO CreateProperty and Properties.Append, read through a refreshed collection.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt1-accdb-properties/`, `fmt1-mdb-properties/`; `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-properties/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/query-props-accdb/` (re-read: Description and an unknown text property appended to a table without error).
- **Open**: none that block; property bytes are W-04.

### R-FMT-2 Ordinary Boolean / Null: a Yes/No field cannot hold Null, and empty text is distinct from Null

- **Claim**: a Yes/No value is -1 or 0; `UPDATE ... SET Y = Null` returns success and reads back False; an insert that omits the Yes/No column stores False, and a Yes/No column added to an existing table reads False for old rows; a Null Yes/No in a compound key is stored as False and a Seek with a Null Yes/No key component fails with 0x800A0C5A. For Text, `''` and Null are distinct and independent: `V = ''`, `V = Null`, `V = 'filled'` and `N = ''` each read back as written after a read-only reopen. For Binary and Long Binary an empty array reads back as Null.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, SQL UPDATE and recordset writes, read after each step.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/bool-ladder/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2-var-null-empty-ladder/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/create-mix/`, `mix-order-b/`, `mix-seek-b/`; `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-yesno/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/binary-bytes-accdb/`, `mask-boundary-accdb/` (the appended Yes/No reads False). Discriminators: Null set by UPDATE, omitted by INSERT, attempted in a key, and appended by ALTER.
- **Open**: the stored value-bit versus presence-bit distinction is a byte question (T).

### R-FMT-2 Scalar boundaries: out-of-range values are refused with error 3421, in-range extremes are stored exactly, fractions round half to even, Money rounds half to even for SQL literals

- **Claim**: a VT_R8 put into a Byte, Integer or Long column rounds half to even and refuses what then lies outside the type with 0x800A0D5D (3421, "Data type conversion error"): Byte 0.5, 1.5, 2.5, -0.4, -0.5 store 0, 2, 2, 0, 0; 255.4 stores 255 and 255.5 is refused; Integer 32767.4 stores 32767, 32767.5 and -32768.6 and an I4 of 32768 or -32769 are refused, -32768.5 stores -32768; Long 2147483647.4 stores 2147483647, 2147483647.5, 2147483648 and -2147483648.6 are refused, -2147483648.5 stores -2147483648, and 1.5, 2.5, 3.5 store 2, 2, 4. Single 3.4028235E38 and 1E-45 are stored, 3.5E38 and -3.5E38 are refused, 1E-46 underflows to 0 without error; Double 1.7976931348623157E308 is stored, the BSTR "1e309" and "-1e309" are refused, "5e-325" underflows to 0. BigInt (ACCDB) stores -1, 9223372036854775807 and -9223372036854775808 and refuses 9223372036854775808 and -9223372036854775809. The refused row stays as an ID-only row. NaN and infinities are accepted for Single and Double and kept by bit pattern. A GUID given as an unbraced string or a 16-byte array is refused with 3421. Currency: SQL numeric literals, `CCur(literal)` and the exact Currency range agree: the ties 0.00005, 0.00015, 0.00025, 0.00035, 0.00045, 0.00055 store 0, 0.0002, 0.0002, 0.0004, 0.0004, 0.0006, and -0.00005, -0.00015, -0.00025 store 0, -0.0002, -0.0002 (half to even at the fifth decimal), 0.00004999 stores 0 and 0.00005001 stores 0.0001; 922337203685477.58075 rounds to ...5808 and is refused as out of range, -922337203685477.58085 rounds to ...5808 and is stored. A VT_R8 put of the same amounts stores 0.0001, 0.0001, 0.0003, 0.0003, 0.0004, 0.0006 (not half to even), and refuses both beyond-range amounts.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB (the integer, Single, Double and Byte rows are identical in both; BigInt ACCDB only), table-recordset puts and SQL `INSERT`, each with a marked Errors read.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/scalar-bounds-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: stored values and 24 refusals each), `money-ties-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/scalar-admission/`; `evidence/r-sort-3/create-nums-accdb/`, `create-nums-mdb/`. Discriminators: fraction just below, at and above each tie and each limit, both signs; three routes for Money (literal, CCur, VT_R8).
- **Open**: why the VT_R8 route gives 1, 1, 3, 3, 4, 6 at the fifth decimal (consistent with rounding the binary value, not tested); VT_CY and BSTR routes for Money; the boundary inside Single's subnormal range.

### R-FMT-2 NUMERIC (public side): a Decimal column keeps at most 28 digits, truncates extra scale toward zero and refuses extra integer digits with error 3761

- **Claim**: ADO DDL creates DECIMAL(p,s) for p up to 28 (the forms (1,0), (2,1), (5,2), (18,4), (28,0), (28,28), (28,10) were created) and refuses (29,0), (38,0) and (38,10) in both formats. A value with more scale than the column is not rounded but truncated toward zero: in (1,0) 0.5, 1.5, 2.5, -0.4 store 0, 1, 2, 0; in (2,1) 0.25, 0.35, 0.05, 0.15 store 0.2, 0.3, 0, 0.1; in (5,2) 12.345, 12.355, 12.365, 0.005, 0.015, 0.025, 0.001 store 12.34, 12.35, 12.36, 0, 0.01, 0.02, 0; in (18,4) 0.00005 and 0.00015 store 0 and 0.0001; in (28,10) 0.00000000005 stores 0. A value with too many integer digits is refused with error 3761 ("The decimal field's precision is too small to accept the numeric you attempted to add"): 10 into (1,0), 10.0 into (2,1), 1000 into (5,2), 100000000000000 into (18,4), 1 into (28,28), a 19-digit integer into (28,10), a 29-digit integer and 79228162514264337593543950335 into (28,0); a 28-digit integer 9999999999999999999999999999 is stored. -0.00 reads "0".
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB (the grid records are identical apart from file size), ADO DDL then DAO writes by SQL literal (at most 15 significant digits) and BSTR integer text, each with a marked Errors read.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/decimal-grid-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: creation results, Precision and Scale properties, the inserts and their errors); `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2-scalar-admission-decimal/`, `fmt2-fx-fixture/`. Discriminators: precision and scale varied one at a time; value just inside and just outside each limit.
- **Open**: whether a negative zero is stored (CStr cannot show it); input by BSTR text with a decimal point (locale 7177); the 17-byte layout, sign and word order are bytes (T).

### R-FMT-2 Dates / GUID (public side): DATE doubles are stored and read back bit-exact within DAO, and GUIDs accept only the braced text form

- **Claim**: VT_DATE puts of -657434, -1.5, -1, -0.5, 0, 0.5, 1.5, 2958465.99998843, 2958466, 2958466.5, 2958467, 10000000, -657435 and -700000 are accepted with no error and read back unchanged (values past 9999-12-31 are admitted); -0 and +0 are kept as different values. A GUID field accepts a braced string, reads back as `{guid {...}}`, and refuses an unbraced string and a 16-byte array with 3421.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO VT_DATE and BSTR puts, each its own write.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2-scalar-admission-date-range/`; `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/scalar-admission/`; `evidence/r-sort-3/create-nums-accdb/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/neg-zero-accdb/` (-0 and +0 are distinct values in GROUP BY, DISTINCT and the index).
- **Open**: `CDbl(T) = 0` on a Date column returned no row in `neg-zero-accdb` (not understood; the Date bits are not in question); GUID word order in the stored bytes (T).

### R-FMT-2 Extended Date/Time (public side): DATETIME2 keeps seven fractional digits, rounds the eighth half up, and refuses year 0 and year 10000

- **Claim**: a DATETIME2 column accepts ISO strings by a recordset BSTR put and by a SQL string literal, with identical results by both routes. 0001-01-01 00:00:00.0000000 and .0000001 are stored; 9999-12-31 23:59:59.9999999 is stored. The eighth digit rounds half up: .00000005 gives .0000001, .12345665 gives .1234567 (half to even would give ...66), .12345675 gives .1234568, .12345674 gives .1234567, .12345676 gives .1234568; .99999995 carries to the next second (:06.0000000) and .99999994 gives .9999999. 9999-12-31 23:59:59.99999995 and .99999999 are accepted and read back as the out-of-range text "12/31/9999 24:00:00.0000000". The strings for year 0 (0000-12-31 23:59:59.9999999) and year 10000 are refused: the put with 0x80020009 (the row stays Null), the SQL literal with error 3464 ("Data type mismatch in criteria expression"). `CStr(V)` of a DATETIME2 value fails with 0x800A0BFF.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB; the string forms only (no VT_DATE route asked).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/dt2-ticks-accdb/` (re-read: 13 stored values per route, rows 14, 15 and 114, 115 refused). Discriminators: the eighth digit varied 4, 5, 6 and the ties 5 at even and odd seventh digits; the two input routes.
- **Open**: the index and ORDER BY of DATETIME2 do not give chronological order (see the R-SORT-3 note on typed keys); VT_DATE input; the 42-byte grammar and key transform are bytes (T).

### R-FMT-2 Text grammar (public round trip, corrected): Text and Memo store UTF-16 units as given, including NUL and lone surrogates, within the declared length, but a leading U+FEFF is not reliably kept

- **Claim**: "wide €", a mixed narrow/wide string, a surrogate pair, a pair written reversed, a lone high and a lone low surrogate (alone and between letters), "a" NUL "b", U+0001, 254 letters plus a lone high surrogate, 253 letters plus a pair and letters around a pair are stored and read back with exactly the stored units, in Text and Memo, ACCDB and MDB (the bytes `00d8`, `00dc`, `00dc00d8` and `610000d86200` are read back; LEN counts each lone unit as 1 and a pair as 2; AscW of the lone surrogates reads -10240 and -9216). 50 narrow or 50 wide characters are accepted and 51 narrow characters are refused with 0x800A0C5B (3163). A string of U+FEFF followed by "x" reads back as "x" with LEN 1 in both columns and both formats, while U+FEFF followed by "bom first" read back intact in `text-grammar`; the lone-U+FEFF case reads back empty (R-STRING-1). An SQL UPDATE of a Text(20) column with 25 characters is not refused and stores the first 20.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, counted BSTR puts through a table recordset; stored units read through SQL AscW and LEN and through a recordset.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/text-units-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: identical in both formats, row 7 reads "x"); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/text-grammar/`, `alt-drop-ladder/`; `evidence/r-text-fffe/`. Discriminators: lone versus paired surrogates, each position; U+FEFF followed by a short and by a long string.
- **Open**: which following content keeps a leading U+FEFF (a compression-header explanation is a guess, not tested); compression, segments and the exact grammar are bytes (T).

### R-FMT-2 Binary and OLE (public side): every byte value round-trips and an empty array reads back as Null

- **Claim**: a Long Binary value of all 256 byte values (256 bytes), one of 4000 patterned bytes, a Binary(255) of bytes 0 to 254, 255 zero bytes and one 0xFF byte read back with exactly the bytes written; empty arrays written to Binary and to Long Binary read back Null; a Null stays Null; a 256-byte array into Binary(255) is refused with 3163.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, table-recordset puts of VT_ARRAY|VT_UI1, read back through a recordset and through `IS NULL`.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/binary-bytes-accdb/` (re-read in the JSON: hex lengths 510 and 512 for the 255- and 256-byte arrays, ending `fdfeff`); `evidence/r-fmt-1/read-*-binary/`, `read-*-longbinary/`. Discriminators: the empty array versus Null; the length limit.
- **Open**: Long Binary forms and chains are bytes (T).

### R-FMT-2 Generated values: AutoNumber takes its value at AddNew or insert, consumes it on cancel, refusal and rollback, follows an explicit ID even when refused, and is reset only by Compact & Repair; random and GUID routes through DAO are refused

- **Claim**: after deleting the last rows, a middle row or all rows, the next insert gets the highest key ever issued plus one (11 for ten rows). A recordset `AddNew` already shows its ID (4 for a table holding 1 to 3); `CancelUpdate` consumes it (the next insert gets 5); an INSERT refused for a duplicate in another unique index consumes one (the next insert after it gets 7, where 6 was taken); an `Update` refused for a duplicate keeps the ID shown at AddNew and the retry stores the row under that ID; a rolled-back transaction consumes its value (9 taken in the transaction, the next insert gets 10); an explicit ID sets the counter even when the insert is refused (an explicit duplicate ID 3 refused with error 3022 made the next insert get 4). After an explicit ID 100 the next automatic ID is 101; `ALTER TABLE ... COUNTER(200, 5)` returns hr 0 and the next two inserts get 200 and 205. DBEngine.CompactDatabase resets the counter to the highest remaining key plus one: after deleting rows 8 to 10 the next insert into the compacted file gets 8, after deleting all rows it gets 1, and a table with rows up to 11 gets 12 (before compaction the same deletion gave 11). A DAO GUID field with Attributes 16 is refused at `TableDefs.Append` with 3001, `G UNIQUEIDENTIFIER DEFAULT GenGUID()` and `G GUID DEFAULT GenGUID()` are refused with 3292 and 3290, and a `NewValues` property of Text "Random" appended to an AutoNumber field is accepted (a second append fails with 3367) but the next five inserts get 1 to 5.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, SQL INSERT without the ID and `@@IDENTITY` after each; Workspaces(0) for the transaction.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/auto-compact-accdb/`, `auto-cancel-fail-accdb/`, `auto-guid-random-accdb/` (re-read: IDs and `@@IDENTITY` as listed); `evidence/r-fmt-2b/au-after-tail-delete/`, `au-after-middle-delete/`, `au-after-all-deleted/`, `au-explicit-high-ladder/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2b-au-explicit-high-ladder/`, `fmt2b-au-reseed-ladder/`. Discriminators: cancel, refused insert, refused update, rollback and explicit duplicate ID each in its own step with the counter read after each; compaction against a no-compaction control (AuK).
- **Open**: a random AutoNumber and a GUID AutoNumber were not reached (the DDL route through ADO and Access remain; T for Access); GUID generation itself is therefore unobserved.

### R-FMT-2 II-05 Statistics word (public DistinctCount): DistinctCount is a counter maintained at insertion, not the number of distinct keys

- **Claim**: an index's DAO `DistinctCount` rises by one when an entry whose key is not yet in the index is inserted and does not rise for a key already present (an index on a column holding one value stays 1 through 1100 rows); it does not fall when rows are deleted (601 rows still read 602, 302 rows still 602) or when the last row of a key is deleted; deleting a key and inserting it again raises it again; an UPDATE that changes the key, to a new key and back, does not change it; a refused insert still raises the indexes ahead of the refusing one (a primary key and a non-unique index went up, the unique index that refused did not); AddNew with CancelUpdate and a refused UPDATE change nothing; the value is the same after a read-only reopen. CompactDatabase recomputes the exact value (3 rows remained after the deletes and refusals: the compacted file reads 2, 3 and 3 for the three indexes, against 4, 6 and 5 before). ACCDB and MDB give the same sequence.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, Indexes refreshed before each read, a primary key, a non-unique and a unique index, SQL and recordset writes.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/dc-rules-accdb/` and `-mdb/` (re-read: the counts after each of 11 steps, after reopen and after compaction are identical in both formats); `evidence/r-fmt-2b/ix-delete-max-key/`, `ix-delete-low-range/`, `ix-delete-reinsert-ladder/`, `ix-dup-ladder/`, `dr-reinsert-same-key-same-values/`, `compact-after-index-growth/`; `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/idx-key-ladder/`, `upd-indexed-value/`. Discriminators: duplicate, delete of a duplicate, delete of the last row of a key, reinsert, update away and back, refused insert, cancel and refused update each a separate step.
- **Open**: refusal order for indexes other than these three; the physical word is bytes (T); `DistinctCount` is never used as a row count.

### R-FMT-2 Compact & Repair diagnostic and R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-COMPACT: CompactDatabase leaves the source unchanged and the copy reads the same public data, except for the counters it recomputes

- **Claim**: `DBEngine.CompactDatabase(source, work file)` returns hr 0, leaves the source file's hash unchanged, and the compacted file reads the same typed rows, field metadata and index lists as the source in eight probes; compacted sizes vary (475136 B from 503808 B after a Memo shrink, equal after growth). It recomputes two counters: the index `DistinctCount` and the AutoNumber seed (see their notes). In the catalog view the only differences are `Database.Name` and, later, `LastUpdated` of some tables and their Containers documents.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (MDB for the statistics probe), work file written next to the attempt, source closed.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2b/compact-after-fixed-update/`, `compact-after-page-growth/`, `compact-after-page-emptied/`, `compact-after-memo-shrink/`, `compact-after-memo-growth/`, `compact-after-ole-insert/`, `compact-after-index-growth/`; `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-compact/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/fmt2b-compact-after-fixed-update/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/auto-compact-accdb/`, `dc-rules-accdb/`, `dc-rules-mdb/`.
- **Open**: Compact after a rolled-back or refused operation; repair-marked Compact & Repair (Access).

### R-FMT-2 Failed operations (refusal identity): duplicate, validation, NOT NULL and foreign-key refusals have stable numbers

- **Claim**: a duplicate in a unique or primary index is refused with 0x800A0BCE (3022), a Null in an index that disallows Nulls or in a primary key with 3058, a Null in a NOT NULL column with 3314, a validation-rule violation with 3316 and the rule's ValidationText ("P must be positive"), a missing parent row with 3201 ("The related record is required in table 'Parent'"). An omitted column takes its default.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, `Execute` with dbFailOnError and per-statement Errors read.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/failed-ladder/`, `pres-txn-failed-ladder/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-unique-corpus-accdb/`, `sort3-np-accdb/`, `sort3b-nulls-refusals/`, `sort3b-dao-index-flags/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/fail-hash-unique/` (3022), `fail-hash-fk/` (3201), `fail-hash-validation/` (3316), `dao-index-primary-accdb/`. Discriminators: each kind on its own statement; both formats.
- **Open**: none that block.

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-DATA-EDITS: ordinary data edits leave every DAO-visible catalog object and property untouched

- **Claim**: a row edit, a row growth that relocates it, an LV replacement in a chain, an LV delete with retirement and a pressure insert of 400 rows change the file hash and change nothing in the DAO catalog view (table, query, relation and Containers names, properties, `LastUpdated`), apart from the edited table's `RecordCount`. A read/write open and close with no statement leaves the hash and the view identical.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, an engine-created baseline with tables, relations, saved queries, user and unknown properties; the view taken before and after.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-row-edit/`, `pres-grow-relocate/`, `pres-lv-replace-chain/`, `pres-lv-delete-retire/`, `pres-ins-pressure/`, `pres-noop/` (pres4 attempts; the pres3 ones are stale); `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/noop-rw-accdb/`. Discriminator: the same view does show property and DDL edits (next note).
- **Open**: protected-range byte comparison (W-04, T); an Access-created front-end baseline (T).

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-PROPERTIES and PRES-CATALOG (public view): each property edit and each DDL edit shows as exactly its own change

- **Claim**: four property edits made one at a time are each visible in the view and change no other property and not `T.LastUpdated`; `CREATE INDEX` grows the file 4096 B and changes nothing visible; add and drop column change only the changed table's `LastUpdated`; `CREATE TABLE` grows the file 12288 B, adds one TableDef, one Containers document, and increments the MSysACEs and MSysObjects record counts.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB; DAO property edits and SQL DDL each as its own step, view before and after.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-property-ladder/`, `pres-catalog-ladder/` (pres4 attempts).
- **Open**: none that block.

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-QUERY-PROPS: a row-returning single-table SELECT lists the table's properties among its own; a join and an action query do not

- **Claim**: with a table T that has two table properties (`Description` and an unknown `CalcDbUnknown`), the saved queries `SELECT * FROM T`, with a WHERE, with ORDER BY, `SELECT DISTINCT` and an aggregate (`COUNT(*)`) each list 16 properties including both, while a join of T and U, an UPDATE of T and a SELECT over U (which has none) list 14 properties without them.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, DAO `CreateQueryDef`, Properties of each QueryDef enumerated after Refresh.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/query-props-accdb/` (re-read: names and counts); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-property-ladder/`. Discriminators: WHERE, ORDER BY, DISTINCT, aggregate, join, action query each as its own query.
- **Open**: a single-table SELECT over a table that has properties other than T (only one such table was asked).

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-RENAME: an engine-only table rename follows into relationships but not into saved queries

- **Claim**: renaming a table through `TableDef.Name` updates the `Table` of a relationship that references it (R1 to R1x, R5 to R5x) and leaves every saved query's SQL unchanged, so a query over the renamed table then cannot be opened (`OpenRecordset` of `SELECT * FROM R3;` fails after R3 is renamed, and the one over R5 after R5); renaming a table that nothing references changes nothing else; the queries over tables not yet renamed still open.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, each of four renames its own step (referenced by a relationship only, by a query only, by nothing, by both); relations, query SQL, table names and an open of each query read after each step.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/rename-variants-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/pres-rename-referenced/`. Discriminators: the four referencing kinds one at a time.
- **Open**: Access Name AutoCorrect (Access only; T).

### R-PRESERVE-1 PRES-ROLLBACK-FAILED: a refusal after index work and a rolled-back insert change the file bytes; a validation refusal, a NOT NULL refusal and a rolled-back update do not

- **Claim**: on copies of one fixture, a refused duplicate in a unique index and a refused foreign-key insert each change the file hash (size constant), the same refused statement again changes it again, and an open and close afterwards changes nothing; a refused validation-rule insert changes nothing, twice. A transaction that inserts one row and is rolled back changes the hash, a rolled-back bulk insert of 3600 rows of about 200 characters changes it again (size constant, row count 0); the committed bulk insert grows the file from 282624 B to 1945600 B. The earlier results fit: the NOT NULL and validation refusals left the hash equal, and a rolled-back UPDATE left it equal.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, copies of one fixture, each step a read/write open, the statement, close and a hash; Workspaces(0) for the transactions.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/fail-hash-unique/`, `fail-hash-fk/`, `fail-hash-validation/`, `rollback-size-accdb/` (re-read: hashes 7fdc... then 57af..., c2fd... for the unique refusal; e133..., 7c52... for the foreign key; unchanged for validation; 19ed..., f910... for the rollbacks); `evidence/r-fmt-2-pres-1/failed-ladder/`, `pres-txn-failed-ladder/`, `txn-ladder/`. Discriminators: refusal kind varied with the fixture fixed; insert versus update rollback; one row versus 3600.
- **Open**: the cause (a statistics word or counter written by the index work is the candidate that fits all records; needs page images, W-04, T).

### R-SORT-3 Descriptor dispatch (file version): dbVersion 64 and 128 files share one order, a dbVersion 256 file orders supplementary-plane characters differently

- **Claim**: with the same locale, corpus and index, the dbVersion 64 MDB and the dbVersion 128 ACCDB give identical GROUP BY classes, ORDER BY order and index walks (18 of 40 values, among them the supplementary-plane characters U+1D11E, U+1D400, U+1F600, U+20000, U+20001, U+10000, U+10FFFF, U+3400, U+9FFF, U+FFFD, U+FFFF, U+D7FF, U+1E9E and the lone surrogates, equal the empty string); the dbVersion 256 file ("14.0") gives the supplementary-plane characters, U+3400, U+E000 and the lone surrogates their own classes, while "", " ", U+9FFF, U+FFFD, U+FFFF, U+D7FF and U+1E9E still equal one another; the order differs correspondingly. In all three files an indexed table and an unindexed copy agree, and CollatingOrder is 1033.
- **Conditions**: one 41-value corpus, files made with 64, 128 and 256, the same locale; reads by GROUP BY, ORDER BY K, ID, and index walks forward and backward.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v64-corpus-mdb/`, `v128-corpus-accdb/`, `v256-corpus-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-sort-3/create-version-256/`, `equality-accdb/`. Discriminators: file version varied with everything else fixed; indexed versus unindexed.
- **Open**: which other characters differ between 128 and 256 (only this corpus); General97 and General - Legacy selection (T).

### R-SORT-3 Text levels: General Text keys fall into stable equality classes, ordered by a compare that SQL, Seek, GROUP BY and unique indexes share (dbVersion 64 and 128)

- **Claim**: on a General-sorted dbVersion 64 or 128 file, SQL `=`, GROUP BY, DISTINCT, Seek, a unique index and the index walk partition Text values into the same classes: case and kana/width variants are one class (A, a, U+FF21, U+FF41; U+3042, U+30A2, U+FF71), é = É = e + U+0301, ligatures equal their expansions (U+FB01 = "fi", U+0153 = "oe", æ = "ae"), "ss" = "SS" but ß is distinct from "ss", è, å, ö, ø, ü, ı and İ are their own classes, Null and "" are separate. Order is Null first, then the class order (" a" < "1" < "10" < "2" < a-class < å < "a b" < aa < ab < "a-b" < ae-class < b < c < ch < cz < d < e < é-class < è < ... < ss-class < u < ü < ue < z < kana < Han), identical on an indexed and an unindexed table, between fresh and incrementally maintained indexes, between ACCDB and MDB and between 0x0409 and 0x0809. A unique index keeps the first row of each class. Within a class the index walk lists rows in insertion order forward and the exact reverse backward; SQL ORDER BY K without ID lists a class's rows in the reverse of insertion order and ORDER BY K DESC keeps that same relative order (see the tie-order note).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB with General collation; DAO table walks, SQL, ADO; two corpora (203 rows with 125 classes; 54 rows with the card's list).
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/equality-accdb/`, `equality-mdb/`, `order-index-*/`, `order-sql-*/`, `seek-*/`, `maintain-*/`, `ado-order-seek-accdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/lg-walk/`, `lg-sql-order/`, `lg-unique-survivors/`, `lu-*/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-unique-corpus-*/`, `sort3b-unique-refusals-lg/`, `-lu/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v64-corpus-mdb/`, `v128-corpus-accdb/` (indexed equals unindexed, MDB equals ACCDB). Discriminators: indexed versus unindexed, fresh versus incremental, unique versus non-unique, two locales, two formats.
- **Open**: LIKE depends on the index (own note). Weights and bytes are T.

### R-SORT-3 Positions and controls (public equality): NUL and trailing spaces carry no weight, most other controls and white space do

- **Claim**: in a General key (dbVersion 128) an embedded, leading or trailing U+0000 is stored as given but ignored ("a" NUL "b" = "ab"; "a" NUL and NUL "a" equal "a"); trailing ordinary spaces are ignored ("a", "a ", "a  ") while a leading space or a leading tab is a different key sorting before "a"; U+0001, U+001F, U+007F after "a", the soft hyphen, a no-break space, tab, LF, CR, CR LF, a zero-width space, U+2028 and U+3000 after "a" each make their own class (the controls sort just after "a", then the soft hyphen; the ideographic space, "a b", the no-break space, tab, LF, CR and U+2028 sort among the punctuation before "ab"); a decomposed acute equals the precomposed one and case ("a" + U+0301 = "A" + U+0301 = á); two different combining marks give the same class in either order; a leading combining mark is its own class. An indexed table and an unindexed copy agree.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (controls probe) and ACCDB/MDB (equality probes), counted BSTRs.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/controls-accdb/` (re-read: 32 values, GROUP BY classes and ORDER BY on both tables); `evidence/r-sort-3/equality-accdb/`, `equality-mdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/create-lg/`. Discriminators: each unit appended to the same base letter one at a time.
- **Open**: which unit advances a position or sets the secondary weight is bytes (T).

### R-SORT-3 UTF-16 boundaries (field length and stored units): the 255-unit Text limit counts UTF-16 units and lone surrogates are stored as given

- **Claim**: a Text(255) key of 255 UTF-16 units (253 letters plus a pair, or 254 letters plus one unit, including a lone high surrogate) is accepted and one of 256 units is refused with 3163; the refused row stays as an ID-only row. A pair counts two units, a lone surrogate one (LEN), and the stored units are the units written. A Seek `=` for a 256-unit key gives NoMatch. In dbVersion 64 and 128 files A x 253 equals A x 253 + U+20000 and A x 254 equals A x 254 + U+D840, while A x 253, A x 254 and A x 255 are mutually distinct; in a dbVersion 256 file the supplementary character has weight (see Descriptor dispatch, file version).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, counted BSTR puts through a table recordset.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/equality-accdb/`, `equality-mdb/`, `seek-accdb/`, `create-corpus-*/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/text-units-accdb/`, `-mdb/` (rows 9 and 10: LEN 255); `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/v256-corpus-accdb/`.
- **Open**: mixed row compression is bytes (T).

### R-SORT-3 Typed key order (Integer and Money, Floating and DATE, NUMERIC / GUID): typed index walks follow numeric order with Nulls first; -0 and +0 are separate keys, and a -0 predicate depends on the index

- **Claim**: forward walks of single-column indexes list Null rows first and then Byte 0, 1, 127, 128, 255; Integer -32768 to 32767; Long minimum to maximum; Currency in numeric order; Decimal(18,4) in numeric order; Date by its double value (-1.5 < -1 < -0.5 < -0 < +0 < 0.5 < 1.5). Single and Double follow the sign-magnitude IEEE order: the negative-sign NaN first, -Infinity, finite values with -0 before +0, +Infinity, then positive-sign NaNs (Double; Single's one NaN sorts first). GUIDs order by the textual hexadecimal digits left to right: 16 GUIDs with the byte 0x01 at each of the 16 byte positions rank from the last textual byte (smallest) to the first (largest), a byte 0x80 at a position ranks just above 0x01 at the same position, all-zero is smallest and all-FF largest, and SQL ORDER BY G gives the same order. -0 and +0 are two values in GROUP BY, DISTINCT, ORDER BY and the index, but without an index the predicates `D = 0`, `D > 0` and `D < 0` treat them as equal (`= 0` returns both rows, `< 0` none), and with an index on D `= 0` returns only +0 and `< 0` returns -0; Single compares them equal (no index). The backward walks of Double and Currency are the exact reverses. ACCDB and MDB agree.
- **Conditions**: one index per column built after the rows, table-recordset walk reading ID; values by bit pattern; the -0 probe reads the same predicates before and after `CREATE INDEX`.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/create-nums-accdb/` (re-read: the forward walks and the backward IX_D and IX_C), `create-nums-mdb/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-nums-decimal-accdb/`, `-mdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/mix-order-n/`, `mix-order-r/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/guid-order-accdb/` (re-read: walk 1 17 23 16 15 14 ... 2 18 24 and the same SQL order), `neg-zero-accdb/` (re-read). Discriminators: NaN of both signs and -0 separately; every GUID byte position one at a time; the same predicate before and after the index.
- **Open**: the DATETIME2 (extended date) index does not order chronologically: with values written by two routes the walk and ORDER BY V give the order 14 15 | 101 102 103 1 2 3 | 104..110 | 111..113 | 4..10 | 11..13, rows of the SQL-literal route ahead of equal or later values of the put route; the cause is not established and needs key bytes (T). Descending numeric indexes; key bytes and the legacy versus corrected NUMERIC mode are T.

### R-SORT-3 Null policy / aliases (public): a Null never collides, an all-Null tuple can be left out of the index, and the refusals are 3022 and 3058

- **Claim**: a plain unique index on K keeps every Null-K row; a compound unique (K, N) keeps rows with a Null in either field and refuses only repeated non-null tuples; `IGNORE NULL` on a single column keeps Null rows out of the index walk, on a compound it leaves out only all-Null tuples; `DISALLOW NULL` and a primary key refuse a Null with 3058 (also at creation on existing Null rows, where no index is left); duplicates refuse with 3022; a NOT NULL column refuses with 3314. DAO indexes made by property combination, setting each property only when True: Primary reads back Unique and Required as well; Unique + Required refuses a duplicate (3022) and a Null (3058); Unique + IgnoreNulls admits Nulls and refuses a duplicate; Required alone admits duplicates and refuses Nulls; Unique + Required + IgnoreNulls still refuses Nulls (Required wins); a Primary index appended to a table that already has a primary key is refused with 3283.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (and MDB for the NP tables); refusals read with dbFailOnError and a marked Errors.
