- **Records**: `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3b-nulls-refusals/`, `sort3b-existing-nulls-isolated/`, `sort3b-dao-index-flags/`, `sort3-np-accdb/`, `sort3-np-mdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/nulls-walk-*`, `nulls-survivors/`; `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/dao-index-primary-accdb/` (re-read: seven tables, flags and survivors). Discriminators: each property combination on its own table; before-rows versus after-rows builds.
- **Open**: plan eligibility of IGNORE NULL (needs a plan view, T). Aliases sharing one tree are bytes (T).

### R-SORT-3 Traversal consequences (Seek operators): Seek lands on the first or last row of a group by a fixed rule

- **Claim**: on a Text index with duplicate classes, Seek `=`, `>=` and `<=` land on the first row of the equal group in index order, `>` on the first row of the next group, `<` on the last row of the previous group, `=` on an absent key sets NoMatch (a later read fails with 3021), and `>=` of an absent key lands on the next present key; a Long, Integer, Byte, Double, Single or Currency key equal to 1 finds the text key "1", Empty and Null find the Null row, Boolean True and Date 1.5 with `=` give NoMatch, two keys on a one-column index give 0x800A0CB2. ADO adSeekFirstEQ lands on the lowest class ID, adSeekLastEQ the highest. A Null component for a Yes/No key raises 0x800A0C5A.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (and MDB twin), table-type recordset on a named index, ADO 10.0 over Microsoft.ACE.OLEDB.12.0 for the ADO lane. The "first row of the group" is the first in the index's tie order, that is insertion order (tie-order note); the earlier "lowest ID" equals it only because those tables were filled in ID order.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3/seek-accdb/`, `seek-mdb/`, `ado-order-seek-accdb/`; `evidence/r-sort-3b/lg-seek-ops/`, `lg-seek-coercion/`, `lu-seek-ops/`, `mix-seek-*/`. Discriminators: each operator and key type separately; descending index varied.
- **Open**: Seek on a long key (the earlier skipped `>=` for the é x 255 group) belongs to the Length510 note; cursor state transitions are R-CURSOR-1.

### R-SORT-3 Traversal consequences (tie order): equal keys walk in insertion order, backward in the reverse, and SQL ORDER BY lists them in the reverse of insertion order

- **Claim**: with equal keys inserted with IDs in a scrambled order (5, 3, 9, 1, 7 for key "a"; 8, 2, 6 for "b"), the index walk lists 5 3 9 1 7 8 2 6 4 (insertion order within each key), the backward walk is the exact reverse, an index built before the rows and one built after give the same walk, and SQL `ORDER BY K` on the indexed tables and on an unindexed copy lists 7 1 9 3 5 6 2 8 4 (each key's rows in reverse insertion order) with `ORDER BY K DESC` keeping the same relative order inside a key (4 6 2 8 7 1 9 3 5) and `ORDER BY K, ID` ordering by ID. After deleting IDs 3 and 7 and inserting IDs 3 and 11 (key "a"), the walk is 5 9 1 3 11: the surviving rows keep their order and the new rows follow in insertion order. ACCDB and MDB give identical sequences. ID plays no part.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB and MDB, SQL inserts in the stated order, walk by table-recordset on the named index.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/tie-order-accdb/`, `tie-order-mdb/` (re-read: the walk and SQL sequences, identical in both formats); `evidence/r-sort-3/maintain-*/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3b-image-drop-rebuild/`. Discriminators: insertion order scrambled against ID order; index before versus after the rows; delete and reinsert.
- **Open**: whether a reinserted row can take the position of a deleted one when pages are reused (not seen here); SQL's reverse-insertion tie order on a larger table.

### R-SORT-3 Compound framing (ordinary lengths): a compound index orders by its fields in turn, each with its own direction, and the index walk equals SQL ORDER BY modulo ties

- **Claim**: for each of five compound indexes (K,V1), (V1,K), (K desc,V1), (K,V1 desc), (K desc,V1 desc), over keys a, A, b, é and Null and partner values Long, Date, Currency, Double, Yes/No and Byte (including Null, negative, zero and extremes), the forward walk lists the same order as the SQL ORDER BY of the same fields except for ties (same class and same partner value), which the index lists in insertion order (these tables were filled in ID order); the backward walk is the exact reverse; an incrementally built index walks the same as a fresh one. -0 and +0 are different partner keys in the Double table and Seek separates them.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, keys at most 20 units; read-only reopen.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-sort-3b/mix-order-n/` (re-read: the five walks equal their SQL orders modulo the a/A ties), `mix-order-r/`, `mix-order-d/`, `mix-order-c/`, `mix-order-y/`, `mix-order-b/`, `mix-seek-*/`; `evidence/r-fix-fmt-sort/sort3-fr-accdb/`, `-mdb/`.
- **Open**: keys near 255 units (next note). Field-framing bytes are T.

### R-SORT-3 Length510 boundary and Shortened-key collisions (public part): ASCII keys of 255 units never lose a trailing field; keys of é, ß and Han near 255 units lose the order of a trailing field, yet unique indexes never refuse a pair that differs in the full value

- **Claim**: for K = "a" x L in a non-unique index on (K, N), rows inserted N = 2 then N = 1, the walk puts N = 1 first at every L from 100 to 255, in ACCDB and MDB. For é, ß and Han the walk puts N = 1 first up to L = 250 (and for é also at 254, for Han also at 254 and 255) but N = 2 (the earlier inserted row) first at L = 253 and 255 (é), 253, 254 and 255 (ß), 253 (Han): a tie in the stored key resolved by insertion order. A unique index on (K, N) accepts both (K, 1) and (K, 2) at every length and refuses only the true duplicate, and a unique index on K accepts K x (L-1) + 'x' and + 'y' (and the same with the first unit changed) at every length for all four characters, refusing only the exact duplicate (3022). For Han the index order of K x 254 and K x 255 under (K, N) is reversed (the longer string first), whereas under unique K it is correct. In SQL, `A.K = B.K` finds ß x 254 + 'x' and ß x 254 + 'y' equal while `A.K < B.K` finds them ordered and the index walk lists the 'y' row first; no such pair was found for 'a', é or Han.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB (a, é, ß, Han) and MDB (a), General dbVersion 128/64; tables LA (non-unique (K,N)), LB (unique (K,N) plus a duplicate), LC (unique K with last-unit and first-unit changes plus a duplicate), LD (non-unique K, SQL pairs); every refusal read with a marked Errors.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/len-series-a-accdb/`, `len-series-a-mdb-mdb/`, `len-series-e-acute-accdb/`, `len-series-sharp-s-accdb/`, `len-series-han-accdb/` (re-read: 16 refusals each, all 3022 on the duplicate controls; walks and joins as listed); `evidence/r-sort-3/keys-order-index-*/`, `keys-uniqueness-meta-*/`, `keys-seek-*/`. Discriminators: character kind varied with lengths fixed; length varied with character fixed; N-tail order against last-unit differences.
- **Open**: the exact length where the trailing field is lost for é, ß and Han (between 250 and 253; lengths 251 and 252 were not asked), the non-monotone é result at 254, the digest and the retained bytes need key bytes (T); whether Seek `=` and SQL equality report false equals for other expansion characters (only ß x 254 showed it, in a join; F).

### R-SORT-3 Length510 boundary, Memo keys: a Memo index, unique index, GROUP BY and DistinctCount compare only the first 255 units

- **Claim**: a unique index on a Memo column is created on an empty table; two Memo values equal in their first 255 UTF-16 units are one key: m x 255 + 'x' (256 units) collides with + 'y' and with all longer values with the same 255-unit prefix, refused with 3022 (ten rows refused), while m x 254 + 'x' and + 'y' (255 units) and a value changed at the first unit are distinct. The non-unique index on the same 15 rows reads DistinctCount 6, GROUP BY on the Memo column gives the same six classes, and the index walk lists the colliding rows together. A join on a Memo column is refused (0x80020009).
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, ASCII 'm' only, lengths 100, 254, 255, 256, 257, 300, 1000.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/memo-prefix-accdb/` (re-read: refusals for IDs 5 to 14, DistinctCount 6 for IX_M and UX_M, GROUP BY classes); `evidence/r-sort-3b/memo-index-ddl/`, `memo-text255-key/`. Discriminators: change at the last unit versus the first, at 254 and 255.
- **Open**: wide characters (255 units versus 255 characters); Memo values in a compound key.

### R-SORT-3 LIKE-RANGE-01/05: a LIKE result set depends on whether an ascending index on the column exists

- **Claim**: on one table, the same LIKE patterns give different row sets with and without an ascending index on K, in ACCDB: with the index, `K LIKE 'a*'` also returns æ (ID 8) and `'e*'` loses the decomposed é (ID 13) while `'é*'` gains it, `'s*'` gains ß, `'f*'` the fi ligature, `'o*'` the œ ligature (the range of a collation-order scan); without it, the pattern is matched unit by unit. A descending-only index changes nothing; dropping the index restores the first results and creating it again restores the second (a repeat); `'a?'` is the same in all states; `K = 'a'` returns IDs 1 and 2 in all states.
- **Conditions**: ACCDB, one 30-row table whose only changing factor is the index (none, IX_K, none, IX_KD descending, none, IX_K), the same ten patterns after each step.
- **Records**: `evidence/r-q-fmt-sort/like-index-accdb/` (re-read); `evidence/r-sort-3b/lg-like-vs-range/`, `lu-like-vs-range/`, `lg-ado-like/`; `evidence/r-sort-3/like-prefix-accdb/`. Discriminators: the index added and dropped on one table, descending versus ascending, a repeat of the state.
- **Open**: unique indexes and compound indexes with K first; plan views are T.

## R-CAT-1, R-TXN-1, R-LOCK-1 to R-LOCK-4 and R-CRASH-1

### R-CAT-1 CAT-NEW: the Option, not the name, chooses the format; refusals are fixed
- **Claim**: `CreateDatabase` gives Version "12.0" for Option 128, as VT_I2, BSTR "128" or VT_R8 128, and when Option is omitted, for `.accdb` and `.mdb` names alike (an `.mdb` name with 128 also gives "12.0"); Option 64 and 192 (64+128) give "4.0" (`Workspace.CreateDatabase` the same); Option 2 (dbEncrypt alone) fails 3001; 32 fails 3170; an existing destination 3204 (file unchanged); a call inside `BeginTrans` 3246 (no file). CollatingOrder is 1033 for the General string used.
- **Conditions**: DAO `DBEngine` and `Workspace.CreateDatabase`, locale `;LANGID=0x0409;CP=1252;COUNTRY=0`, ACE 16.0.20527.20196.
- **Records**: new/cat-new-argument-forms/ (every form; the .mdb-with-omitted and .mdb-with-128 variants discriminate name from option); evidence/r-cat-1/create-accdb-empty/, create-accdb-option-omitted/, create-accdb-workspace-route/, create-accdb-ext-v40/, create-mdb-empty/, create-mdb-version30/, create-accdb-existing-destination/, create-accdb-in-transaction/.
- **Open**: file sizes differ by version (172,032 B for 12.0, 77,824 B for 4.0, byte classes are W-04); locale strings beyond General, passwords (T).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-B: name rules in every namespace; TableDefs.Item is the odd lookup
- **Claim**: Table, field, index, relation and query names compare case-insensitively and accent-insensitively: duplicate table 3010, field 3191, index 3375, relation 3012, query 3012 (`Tbl`/`TBL`, `Aa`/`aA`, U+00E9/U+00C9 pairs, `IxA`/`ixa`); a leading space and `. ! [ ]` or a backquote fail 3125; a trailing space is kept; 64 characters are accepted, 65 fail 3001 at `CreateTableDef`, `CreateField` and `CreateRelation` but 3125 for an index name. Precomposed U+00E9 and decomposed `e`+U+0301 are one name for Append, `CREATE TABLE` (3010), `SELECT * FROM [..]` and `DROP TABLE`, but `TableDefs.Item("..")` finds only the spelling stored (3265 for the other), in both creation orders.
- **Conditions**: DAO/SQL on ACCDB, collections refreshed.
- **Records**: new/cat-b-names-fields-indexes-relations/, new/cat-b-names-normalization/ (both orders discriminate); evidence/r-cat-1/names/.
- **Open**: MSys rows and IDs (3112, W-04); non-General collations; other accented pairs.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-P: custom properties on every owner, and their rollback
- **Claim**: A custom Long property appended to the Database, a TableDef, a Field, an Index and a QueryDef is read back (Type 4, value) after closing and reopening read-only. Under `BeginTrans`, setting the Database property to 999 and appending a second property are visible in the level; after `Rollback` and `Properties.Refresh` the original value (123456) is back and the new property gives 3270, on the same handle and on a second Workspace. Input grammar: Null/Empty/`""`/empty bytes 3385, missing value 3386, duplicate 3367, type 99 3259, 65-character name 3001, `Type` change 3219, deleting a built-in 3384; Boolean coerces from numbers; types Boolean..GUID round-trip.
- **Records**: new/cat-p-custom-properties-all-owners-rollback/ (all owners; Rollback with Refresh); evidence/r-cat-1/properties/, property-inputs/, property-edits/; evidence/r-cat-1b/field-access-property-append/.
- **Open**: LvProp bytes, flag80 (T).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-Q: saved-query public view and the save-time rendering
- **Claim**: On save, `CreateQueryDef` re-renders SQL: keywords upper-cased, one clause per line with CR LF, a closing `;` plus CR LF, spaces collapsed, identifier case kept (`t.id` stays), `ORDER BY t.id DESC , t.a`, `DELETE` becomes `DELETE *`, `INSERT INTO U ( ID, X )`, a two-predicate `ON T.ID = U.ID AND T.A = U.A` becomes `ON (T.A = U.A) AND (T.ID = U.ID)` (one sample, order reversed), a UNION text is kept as written plus `;`, an embedded tab and a string with `;` survive. The reopened text equals the creating handle's. After reopen `Type` is select 0, union 128, update 48, delete 32, insert 64, crosstab 16 (0 on the creating handle for action queries as before); `ReturnsRecords` -1 for every shape; `Execute` on a select 3065, `OpenRecordset` on an action 3219; getter text set back is stored verbatim until reopen; no U+0000; `PARAMETERS` order fixes Parameters/Fields order; invalid SQL is refused at create (3129/3142/3075).
- **Records**: new/cat-q-sql-reformat-matrix/ (16 shapes, created and reopened); evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/qd-view-writable-reopen/, qd-create-invalid-sql-errors/; evidence/r-cat-1/querydefs/, sql-round-trip/, order-variants/; evidence/r-cat-1b/qd-execute-on-row-returning/, qd-openrecordset-on-action/.
- **Open**: whether the ON reordering is a rule (one sample); MSysQueries Order bytes (W-04); pass-through execution (ODBC forbidden). Not a universal serializer.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-V: held-object lifetime and parameter state
- **Claim**: A held QueryDef whose saved query is deleted answers 3420; recreating the name does not revive it; renaming makes the old name unresolvable (3265); after `Database.Close` held children answer 3420; `Workspace.Close` (also with an open transaction) leaves `Database`, recordset, TableDef, QueryDef and Workspace answering 3420 while `Workspaces.Count` stays 1; `Database.Close` leaves the Workspace usable and its children 3420. `QueryDefs.Item("QP")` twice on one Database returns one shared instance (a value set through one reads through the other); an instance from another Database of the same Workspace or from another Workspace has its own, empty parameter values (open fails 3061 until set). Parameter values persist across executions and after a `SQL` put; a held `Parameters` collection keeps its order after a `SQL` put (reopen reorders).
- **Records**: new/cat-v-two-instances-parameters/ (four instances), new/txn-close-workspace-close-held-objects/ (three Close kinds; resolves evidence/r-def-cat-txn/cat-workspace-close-held-objects/, whose Database was opened through the default Workspace); evidence/r-cat-1/held-querydef-lifetime/, held-sql-replace/, collection-refresh/.
- **Open**: `Recordset.Requery()` after a parameter change (the probe set the same value); `Requery(QueryDef)` crashes ACE and is never run.

### R-CAT-1 CAT-D: relationship semantics through DAO
- **Claim**: Each enforced relation adds its own Foreign index on the child, named like the relation; two relations over the same foreign-key column give two indexes (`R1`, `R2`), no sharing, each enforced independently (an insert needs a parent in both; deleting either parent key gives 3200); `Relations.Delete("R1")` removes its index and its enforcement. Also: relation `Attributes` reads back as created; no-enforce adds no index; orphans 3201 (compound keys, self-reference), parent delete/update 3200 unless cascading (also self-reference and a cycle); `Append` over existing orphans 3201 when enforced; type pairs LONG→LONG, COUNTER→LONG, TEXT10→TEXT20 accepted, others 3368; renames propagate; `DROP TABLE` of a parent 3281, `DROP COLUMN` in a relation 3383, DDL under a held recordset 3211 (same process) or 3262 (other process).
- **Records**: new/cat-d-two-relations-shared-fk-columns/; evidence/r-cat-1b/rel-orphan-insert/, rel-parent-delete-by-attributes/, rel-index-ownership/, rename-field-in-relation/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/cat-drop-table-in-relation/, cat-rel-type-pairs-complete/, rel-orphan-insert-errors/; evidence/r-def-cat-txn/cat-rel-self-reference-cascade/, cat-rel-cascade-cycle/, cat-rel-compound-key-enforcement/.
- **Open**: which relation's message an insert violating both names (one sample names the first); MSys/ACL rows, Name AutoCorrect (T).

### R-CAT-1 CAT-I: saved queries bind by name at each open; rule changes bite at the next Execute
- **Claim**: A saved query holds text only: renames do not rewrite it; the next open fails 3061 (unresolved identifier becomes an implicit parameter) or 3078; dropping a used column succeeds silently and breaks dependents at the next open; the column coming back resolves the parameter; changed nested output changes the outer open; held `Fields`/`Parameters` are snapshots until reopen; wildcard mode follows the client (DAO `*`, ACE ADO `%`). A held saved parameter INSERT and a fresh instance behave the same after each rule change: a field `ValidationRule` gives 3316 with its text, `Required` with Null gives 3314, a unique index with a duplicate gives 3022; a Caption property changes nothing.
- **Records**: new/cat-i-saved-append-after-rule-change/ (held vs fresh instance, four rule kinds); evidence/r-cat-1/select-star/, rename-column/, drop-column/, unresolved-identifier/, nested-querydef/, recreate-table/, column-type-change/, wildcard-mode/; evidence/r-cat-1b/rename-query-dependent-query/.
- **Open**: function registry, sandbox, cache hit/miss (not observable from DAO).

### R-CAT-1 II-11: CHECK, VIEW, PROCEDURE, OWNERACCESS through the ACE OLE DB provider
- **Claim**: ADO (ANSI-92) accepts `ALTER TABLE ADD CONSTRAINT .. CHECK (..)` (DAO refuses CHECK with 3289); the rule is enforced for ADO and DAO inserts (error names the rule `CK_V`; DAO 3317), is not checked against existing rows (`CHECK (V < 20)` over a row V = 25 is accepted), and is not visible as DAO `ValidationRule` of the table or field. A column-level `CHECK` in `CREATE TABLE` is refused (3292, no table). `CREATE VIEW` creates a saved select QueryDef (Type 0); `CREATE PROCEDURE` with a select creates a Type 0 query, with a parameter and an UPDATE a Type 48 query with a `PARAMETERS` line; DAO lists them by name; `EXEC P2 1` runs the update; `WITH OWNERACCESS OPTION` survives in the saved SQL of a view; `DROP VIEW`/`DROP PROCEDURE` remove them.
- **Records**: new/ii11-ado-check-constraint/ (satisfied, violated-by-existing-row, enforced, column-level), new/ii11-ado-view-procedure-owneraccess/; evidence/r-sql-2-3/act-ddl-objects (DAO refusals, per the summary).
- **Open**: SQL CHECK on updates/subqueries, native persistence of the constraint (W-04); secured-workgroup cases are excluded.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-NEST: current-level nesting
- **Claim**: `CommitTrans`/`Rollback` act on the current level; inner commit then outer rollback reverses both; inner rollback then outer commit keeps the outer change; the same row changed at every level takes the outer image after an inner rollback; empty levels raise nothing; resolving with no level raises 3034; the 8th `BeginTrans` raises 3003 (7 levels, also on a second Workspace independently) and writes at levels 6 and 7 raise 3003; `CommitTrans` accepts 0/Missing/Empty/I2 1/UI1 1/R8 1.0/"1", other values 3001 leaving the level open; `BeginTrans(1)` 3001 starts no level; a file opened after a nested begin joins and rolls back with it.
- **Records**: evidence/r-txn-1/nest-inner-rollback/ (+ -mdb), nest-middle-rollback-after-inner-commit/, nest-outer-rollback-after-inner-commits/, nest-same-row-all-levels/, nest-empty-levels/, nest-resolve-without-level/, nest-late-file-b/; evidence/r-txn-1b/nest-depth-reuse-after-commit/, nest-depth-second-workspace/, nest-depth-recordset-writes/ (+ -mdb), commit-option-values/, commit-option-vartypes/, txn-call-argument-counts/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/nest-depth-recordset-writes-fresh-witness/.
- **Open**: capacity of write levels vs statement failure at level 6.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-STMT: statement scope by failure cause
- **Claim**: Earlier changes always survive and later ones apply. With `dbFailOnError`: duplicate key in `INSERT…SELECT` (first/middle/last/only) 3022, validation 3316, RI delete 3200 and FK 3201 leave no candidate changed (`RecordsAffected` 0); an expression error (error 11) and lock exhaustion (3035) leave the candidates processed before the failure changed; a lock-blocked candidate (another process's pessimistic Edit on its page) gives 3260 after about 2.2 s and the candidates before it in scan order stay changed, those after are untouched (blocked first: nothing changed; middle or last: the earlier ones changed; all: nothing). Without the option: duplicates are skipped silently, a validation-blocked row is skipped, division by zero stores Null, a blocked candidate is skipped silently after the same 2.2 s wait (each blocked page costs one wait: 8.9 s for four) and `RecordsAffected` counts only the updated rows. Same for saved and temporary QueryDef and depth 0-2, on MDB.
- **Records**: new/txn-stmt-lock-blocked-positions/ (first, middle, last, all x option/no option); evidence/r-txn-1/stmt-unique-middle-fail-d0/, -d1/, -d2/, -d1-mdb/, stmt-unique-last-fail-d1/, stmt-unique-all-fail-d1/, stmt-unique-middle-plain-d1/, stmt-saved-querydef-fail-d1/, stmt-validation-plain-d1/, stmt-fk-counter-fail-d1/; evidence/r-txn-1b/implicit-*, rb-*; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/stmt-expression-fail-a-survives/.
- **Open**: I/O failure (T); a blocked candidate on the same page as an unblocked one is blocked with it (earlier "whole statement skipped" record, r-lock-3-4-txn/txn-stmt-blocked-no-scope).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-MULTI: files and Workspaces
- **Claim**: Within one Workspace, changes in files A and B resolve together by one `Rollback`/`CommitTrans` in either touch order; a file opened after the first `BeginTrans` joins; a statement failure in B after a change in A is statement-only; closing B inside the level still rolls back both; three handles to one file roll back together. Two Workspaces touching the same two files in opposite order (w1: A then B, w2: B then A) resolve independently: Rollback of one and CommitTrans of the other in either assignment gives each Workspace's changes on both files and nothing from the other, no deadlock; an update colliding with the other Workspace's page (T2 row 3 vs row 1, three rows on one page) is refused 3188. A refused second-file admission (`OpenDatabase` on a file another process holds exclusively, 3045) leaves the level usable: later updates and `CommitTrans` succeed and both main-file changes are committed (independent final read), and the file opens after the other process closes.
- **Records**: new/txn-multi-two-workspaces-opposite-order/, new/txn-multi-second-file-held-exclusive/; evidence/r-txn-1b/multi-a-then-b-rollback/, multi-b-then-a-rollback/, multi-b-fails-after-a-commit/, multi-close-b-in-txn/, multi-same-file-handles/; evidence/r-txn-1/nest-late-file-b/.
- **Open**: an order test whose rows do not share a page.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-LOCAL: lockers inside and across processes
- **Claim**: With a transaction (or pessimistic Edit) on row 1, a write to row 1 or to row 2 (same page) fails after the retry time with 3260 across processes and 3188 across Workspaces of one process, `RecordsAffected` 0, nothing changed; a far row (ID 64) updated through an unindexed field and another table are admitted, and that change survives the first locker's Rollback and CommitTrans; an update of the indexed N on a far row is refused 3188 in-process (an index leaf is a conflict resource); after the holder's resolution the retried write succeeds. The refusal time is `dbLockRetry` x `dbLockDelay` plus about 0.07-0.2 s: default 2.21-2.23 s (3 repeats), retry 1 x delay 1 15 ms (3), retry 20 x delay 250 5.08-5.11 s (2); the three-repeat `dbPageTimeout` 100 step ran with retry 20 and delay 250 still set (5.09 s) and shows no PageTimeout effect.
- **Records**: new/txn-local-conflict-timing/ (timestamped brackets), new/txn-local-inproc-far-rows-and-orders/ (3188 in 7 of 8 phases; other-table phase admitted, A rollback then B commit), new/txn-range-far-row-final-read/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/txn-local-xproc-same-row/, -same-page/, -far-row/, -other-table/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/txn-local-xproc-same-page-retry/, local-two-rows-far-b-commit-a-rollback/.
- **Open**: admitted-case commit/rollback orders other than A:R then B:C and B:C then A:R (in-process far rows through an unindexed field); PageTimeout alone; leaf/map footprints (W-04).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-VIEW: transaction visibility and the refresh boundary
- **Claim**: Uncommitted insert, delete, update and DDL of one Workspace are never visible to another Workspace or process; after a commit another process's held dynaset and table cursors and new snapshots show the value only after a delay, the first look past 4.1-5.6 s after the commit (twice: 5.5-s look in both probes, 4.0-s look still old), the held snapshot stays old until `Requery`, which moves it (and the dynaset) to the new value; with `SetOption(dbPageTimeout, 1000)` the change appeared between 2.1 and 4.1 s (single sample); `Transactions` is True for dynaset/table, False for snapshot/forward-only; a held dynaset shows a deleted row as a placeholder (3167).
- **Records**: new/txn-view-refresh-boundary/, new/read-refresh-grid/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/txn-view-readers-late/, xproc-uncommitted-membership-late/, xproc-held-cursors-membership-late/, xproc-reader-own-level-late/; evidence/r-txn-1b/ws2-reader-after-other-rollback/, rs-types-after-rollback/.
- **Open**: a refresh boundary proportional to PageTimeout (the 1000 ms boundary is not 1 s); each look is an observation that may itself refresh.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-CLOSE: what ends and releases a level
- **Claim**: `Workspace.Close` with 1, 2 or 3 levels (also after an inner commit, with a pending Edit, with two Databases, or with an update inside the level) returns without error, rolls back everything and leaves every held child 3420; releasing Workspace and Database without `Close` also rolls back; `Database.Close` with the level open keeps the level (Rollback works) and leaves children 3420; a pending Edit/AddNew is dropped by Rollback (`EditMode` 0, `Update` 3020) and survives `CommitTrans`. Closing a recordset opened inside the level never raised and never ended the level in eight variants: plain dynaset, Edit+Update, clone closed after the original's Update, original closed with a clone, clone closed, table-type Edit+Update, Edit+CancelUpdate, AddNew+Update; Rollback then succeeded and all values were back.
- **Records**: new/txn-close-recordset-close-variants/, new/txn-close-workspace-close-held-objects/; evidence/r-txn-1b/wsclose-pending-edit-depth-1/, wsclose-two-databases-depth-1/, wsclose-after-inner-commit-depth-2/, wsrelease-no-close-depth-1/; evidence/r-txn-1/workspace-close-depth-1/, workspace-close-depth-3/, close-db-held-recordset/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/wsrelease-no-close-read-first/; evidence/r-def-cat-txn/txn-pending-addnew-*/.
- **Open**: the earlier 3246 on closing a recordset (and a clone) in the level ended the level (evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/clone-close-read-between/, clone-close-update-only/, r-def-cat-txn/txn-close-original-recordset-while-clone-holds-level/): their programs opened the recordset before `BeginTrans`; not reproduced here, so the discriminator (opened before the level) is untested. Callbacks.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH-COMMIT: an explicit commit is in the file at return
- **Claim**: After the outer `CommitTrans` (default and `dbForceOSFlush`) the file has changed (34.1 MB at the call, equal 500 ms later) and a process killed 0 ms after the return leaves the rows visible to the next opener (ACCDB and MDB, 3 of 3); an inner forced commit followed by outer Rollback leaves the file unchanged. A small transaction leaves the main file unchanged until the commit; a large one (4,096-row insert) writes pages before the commit (17.2 → 30.7 MB inside the level), yet a process killed 1.5 s after the Execute, before CommitTrans, leaves the next opener the original 2,048 rows in a 13.0 MB file.
- **Conditions**: process-termination model with a live OS cache; cannot separate default from forced commit or show power-loss durability.
- **Records**: evidence/r-txn-flush/accdb-explicit-wait-0ms/ and mdb-explicit-wait-0ms/ (3 each); evidence/r-crash-1-midcall/*-txn-kill-after-100ms, -400ms; evidence/r-crash-1-kill/*-txn-committed-ace-first, *-txn-committed-forceflush-ace-first; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/txn-flush-live-images/; new/txn-flush-implicit-file-image-timeline/ (txn-inside, txn-committed-0), new/crash-txn-kill-between-execute-and-commit/.
- **Open**: `dbUserCommitSync=No` lost the commit once per format (n=1); a trace for forced vs default (T).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH-OP: an implicit statement is not in the file at return
- **Claim**: An implicit (non-transactional) `Execute` returns before its change is in the file: killed 0 or 250 ms after return the next opener sees the old count (3 of 3 each, ACCDB and MDB), and the file image read in-process after the return is still the old size at 0 and 250 ms.
- **Records**: evidence/r-txn-flush/*-implicit-wait-0ms/, *-wait-250ms/; new/txn-flush-implicit-file-image-timeline/ (t0, t250).
- **Open**: `dbImplicitCommitSync` "Yes" made the rows present at 0 ms (n=1 per format; repeat 5 attempts).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-FLUSH-BEHIND: the write-behind window
- **Claim**: After an implicit `Execute` returns, the main file keeps its post-statement size (13.0 MB) at 0 and 250 ms and has its final image (17.2 MB, the size of the 4,096-row file) at the next look, 500 ms; a process killed 500 ms or 750 ms after the return leaves 4,096 rows (n=1 each), at 1 s 3 of 3, at 250 ms 0 of 3: the window ends in (250, 500] ms. `Database.Close` right after the Execute makes the rows present at a 0 ms kill and the file final at once (n=1); `DBEngine.Idle(dbRefreshCache)` does not (kill at 0 ms: 2,048 rows, n=1). The same window explains why another process's `Idle(dbRefreshCache)` right after five rapid implicit updates shows the fourth, not the fifth (new/read-refresh-grid).
- **Records**: evidence/r-txn-flush/*-implicit-wait-0ms/, -250ms/, -1000ms/ (3 attempts each), *-wait-3000ms/, *-wait-6000ms/; evidence/r-crash-1-midcall/*-implicit-kill-after-100ms, -400ms; new/txn-flush-implicit-kill-after-500ms-return/, -750ms-return/, txn-flush-implicit-file-image-timeline/, txn-flush-implicit-idle-refresh-then-kill/, txn-flush-implicit-db-close-then-kill/, new/read-refresh-grid/.
- **Open**: the boundary inside (250, 500] ms and the Close/Idle effects at n=1 (repeat 3-5 attempts); `sharedasyncdelay-0` unchanged, `flushtimeout-1` mixed.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-TEMP: lock-count exhaustion only
- **Claim**: With `SetOption(dbMaxLocksPerFile, 5)` a bulk `UPDATE` raises 3035 with `RecordsAffected` 0, earlier changes stay, the level stays usable (`CommitTrans` keeps earlier changes, `Rollback` reverses them, `Database.Close` leaves them uncommitted); implicit statements leave partial progress (COUNT 512, SUM 510) with or without `dbFailOnError`.
- **Records**: evidence/r-txn-1/exhaust-locks-commit/, -rollback/, -close/; evidence/r-txn-1b/implicit-exhaustion-failonerror/, implicit-exhaustion-nofail/.
- **Open**: TEMP/disk faults (T).

### R-TXN-1 TXN-DDL: catalog changes are transactional
- **Claim**: CREATE/ALTER/DROP TABLE, index add/drop, DAO TableDef append, SELECT INTO, relation drop, QueryDef create/SQL change and database property create are reversed by Rollback (also an outer rollback after inner commit), judged on a fresh Workspace after `Refresh`; a held `TableDef` shows old counts until `Refresh`; DROP under an open recordset 3211; another Workspace or process never lists uncommitted objects; AutoNumber values consumed in the level are not restored.
- **Records**: evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/ddl-alter-index-drop-rollback-catalog/, ddl-relation-querydef-property-rollback-refresh/, ddl-second-workspace-idle/; evidence/r-txn-1b/ddl-drop-recreate-rollback/, ddl-dao-tabledef-append-rollback/, ddl-inner-commit-outer-rollback/, ddl-held-tabledef-after-rollback/, temp-make-table-rollback/; evidence/r-txn-1/ddl-create-table-rollback/, ddl-nested-failure-outer-rollback/; new/cat-p-custom-properties-all-owners-rollback/ (database properties).
- **Open**: counter restoration across a crash.

### R-TXN-1 TXN-SUPPORT: ADO nesting values (partial)
- **Claim**: Through the ACE OLE DB provider `Connection.BeginTrans` returns the level (1, 2, 3), `Attributes` reads 0; setting `adXactCommitRetaining` (131072) or `adXactAbortRetaining` (262144) is accepted, and the next `BeginTrans` returns a level one higher after a `CommitTrans` (2 → 3, 4 → 5), so the retaining attribute starts a new level; `Close` inside a transaction raises 0x800A0CAE; provider properties `Transaction DDL` 16 and `Isolation Levels` 4096; with the outermost level never resolved no change reached the file (independent DAO read: both rows unchanged).
- **Records**: new/txn-support-ado-nesting/.
- **Open**: every ADO `Rollback` raised 0x800A0BB9 here, unlike evidence/r-txn-1/local-dao-ado-two-rows/ (a control without the open `Execute` recordset); ISAM/ODBC participants (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-OPEN: share admission matrix
- **Claim**: Across processes, DAO exclusive read/write refuses every second mode (3045); exclusive read-only admits exclusive/shared read-only and refuses read/write; shared read-only admits shared read-only/read-write and exclusive read-only, refuses exclusive read/write (3704 ACCDB, 3045 MDB); shared read/write admits shared modes and refuses exclusive; native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` opens beside DAO read-only modes and is refused (Win32 32) beside DAO read/write; native read/write share-zero is refused beside every DAO mode and refuses every DAO mode (3045) in both orders; same pattern in ACCDB and MDB.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/{accdb,mdb}-dao-*-then-dao-* (32 pairs), *-native-r-shareread-then-dao-*, *-native-rw-share0-then-dao-*, *-dao-*-then-native-*.
- **Open**: the 3704/3045 split; actual access/share flags (W-PROBE).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-DEFAULT: omitted arguments are shared read/write
- **Claim**: `OpenDatabase(path)` with every optional argument omitted, in two processes, opens in both and reads in both (2,048 rows); the lock file grows 64 → 128 B; after one closes, a default open by the other still works. Same as the explicit shared read/write pair.
- **Records**: new/ex-default-omitted-arguments/; evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-dao-shared-rw-then-dao-shared-rw.
- **Open**: an exclusive-only open beside a default open across processes (the probe's attempt was admitted in-process after b's own open; EX-PUBLIC rules).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-CREATE: creation beside other processes
- **Claim**: `CreateDatabase` of a path that exists is refused 3204 and does not touch the file, whether the other process created it a moment before (a started 0.25 s earlier, b called during that window), holds it open (exclusive: `OpenDatabase` by the other process 3045) or has closed it; the winning creator's file opens (Version 12.0, 172,032 B) in an independent final read.
- **Records**: new/ex-create-race-two-creators/ (one overlap), new/ex-create-over-held-destination/ (held, then closed); evidence/r-cat-1/create-accdb-existing-destination/.
- **Open**: timing of the race (n=1); cleanup with pending I/O (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-ALIAS: path spellings across processes (partial)
- **Claim**: With A holding the plain path exclusive, B opening the upper-case, `.\` or slash spelling shared read/write is refused 3045 as for the plain path; unheld, those spellings open.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-alias-xproc-upper/, -dot/, -slashes/; new/ex-alias-extended-path-unheld-control/ (plain, slashes, dot, upper open unheld).
- **Open**: the `\\?\` spelling gives 3043 unheld too, but the probe built `\\?\inputs\...` (relative, not a valid extended path); an absolute `\\?\C:\...` spelling is untested.

### R-LOCK-1 EX-PROBE: metadata probing beside every DAO mode
- **Claim**: A `FILE_READ_ATTRIBUTES` open with all sharing succeeds beside every DAO mode (also exclusive read/write); a data read through it gives Win32 5; the lock file opens for read with all sharing when present (64 B shared, absent under exclusive opens, Win32 2).
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/accdb-dao-excl-rw-then-metadata-probe/, mdb-dao-shared-ro-then-metadata-probe/ (representative of 16).
- **Open**: `FileIdInfo` itself is not requested by the driver.

### R-LOCK-1 EX-PUBLIC and R-LOCK-2 RO-INPROC: in-process admission
- **Claim**: In one process (same or second Workspace) a second binding is admitted more widely than across processes: beside an exclusive or shared read/write first binding all four modes open; beside exclusive read-only, exclusive and shared read/write are refused (3045); beside shared read-only, exclusive read/write is refused (3704 ACCDB, 3045 MDB); reads succeed in both, also after the first closes.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/{accdb,mdb}-inproc-ws1-*-then-*, accdb-inproc-ws2-*-then-*.
- **Open**: alias spellings in-process do not discriminate.

### R-LOCK-1 EX-PERM and R-LOCK-2 RO-PERM: file attributes
- **Claim**: With the database read-only, shared and exclusive read/write opens fail 3051, read-only opens succeed (`Updatable` False; lock file 64 B for shared, none for exclusive); with a read-only lock file, shared modes fail (read/write 3051, read-only 3050) and exclusive modes open; with both the database and a 64-byte lock file read-only, shared read-only 3050 and read/write 3051, exclusive read/write 3051 and exclusive read-only opens (count read, `Updatable` False) with the lock file untouched (hash f5a5fd42d16a unchanged); a stale writable lock file stops no mode and is replaced.
- **Records**: new/ex-perm-readonly-db-and-lockfile-exclusive/ (completes the matrix); evidence/r-lock-perm/accdb-db-readonly-all-modes/, accdb-readonly-lockfile-all-modes/, accdb-stale-lockfile-all-modes/, accdb-db-and-lockfile-readonly/ and mdb counterparts.
- **Open**: folder ACLs (T).

### R-LOCK-1 EX-LIFE: what keeps an exclusive file held
- **Claim**: After `Recordset.Close`+`Database.Close` or release without `Close`, a second process opens read/write within about 25 ms. With a table recordset and a TableDef held: releasing only the Database keeps the exclusion (3045); closing the recordset (TableDef still held) ends it; releasing the TableDef changes nothing more. With an open transaction on a created Workspace, the exclusion stays after the Database and recordset are released (3045), ends at `Rollback`, and the rolled-back row reads unchanged in an independent open. A killed exclusive holder leaves no lock file and the next opener opens.
- **Records**: new/ex-life-child-and-transaction-owner/ (seven attempts after seven states); evidence/r-lock-1-2/accdb-life-close/, accdb-life-release/ (+ mdb); evidence/r-crash-1-kill/accdb-kill-open-only-exclusive-ace-first/, mdb-...
- **Open**: pending I/O, inherited handles (T); each state sampled once.

### R-LOCK-2 RO-PAIR: readers coexist
- **Claim**: Two and three shared read-only readers coexist and read (2,048 rows each); exclusive read-only is admitted beside a shared read-only reader (against the book's expectation); the lock file grows 64 → 128 B and is gone when all close.
- **Records**: new/ro-three-readers-and-reads-after-writer/ (a, b, c); evidence/r-lock-1-2/*-dao-shared-ro-then-dao-shared-ro, *-dao-shared-ro-then-dao-excl-ro; evidence/r-lock-perm/accdb-readonly-db-two-readers/, mdb-...
- **Open**: none that blocks.

### R-LOCK-2 RO-ACTUAL: DAO read-only opens hold no database write access
- **Claim**: A DAO shared or exclusive read-only open does not hold write access to the database file: a native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` open succeeds and reads 4096 bytes beside it, and fails Win32 32 beside DAO read/write; read-only readers permit writers (share-write).
- **Conditions**: inferred from Windows share semantics and refusals, not from a handle trace.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-1-2/{accdb,mdb}-dao-shared-ro-then-native-r-shareread/, -dao-excl-ro-then-native-r-shareread/, -dao-shared-rw-then-native-r-shareread/, -dao-excl-rw-then-native-r-shareread/.
- **Open**: direct handle observation (W-PROBE).

### R-LOCK-2 RO-LATE: writers beside readers
- **Claim**: With DAO shared read-only readers, a DAO shared read/write writer opens and updates (`RecordsAffected` 1) while two or three readers are open, after one closes and after all close; the readers then (7 s, Idle) read the writer's value from a new snapshot and see an intact file; a DAO exclusive writer and a native read/write share-zero writer are refused. With native `GENERIC_READ|FILE_SHARE_READ` readers a and b, the DAO shared read/write open and the DAO exclusive open are refused 3045 and the native share-zero open Win32 32 with both held and with b alone held; after both close the writer opens and updates, native share-zero opens, and the update reads back.
- **Records**: new/ro-late-native-lease-then-dao-writer/, new/ro-three-readers-and-reads-after-writer/; evidence/r-lock-1-2/accdb-ro-late/, mdb-ro-late/.
- **Open**: none that blocks (the exclusive-open attempts after the process's own open were admitted in-process and are not evidence).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-ROLE: a pending Edit does not block readers
- **Claim**: While another process holds a pessimistic `Edit` on row 1, readers (snapshot, dynaset, forward-only, table; held and newly opened) read row 1 and the unedited values without error.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-role-edit-existing-readers/, read-role-edit-then-update-new-reader/, write-timing-pessimistic-matrix/.
- **Open**: native lock purpose (T).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-REFRESH: the staleness boundary and Idle
- **Claim**: Another process's committed or implicit update becomes visible to a held dynaset, a held table cursor and a new snapshot between 4.0 and 5.5 s after the update (both new probes: 4.08-s look old, 5.5-s look new), not to a held snapshot until `Requery`; `Idle(dbRefreshCache)` before that time changes nothing. After five rapid implicit updates (+1 each, 100 ms apart), `Idle(dbRefreshCache)` 15 ms after the last shows the fourth value (1014), not the fifth (1015); after 7 s it shows 1015: Idle shows the last flushed state, so the earlier 1001-versus-1002 ladder is the write-behind window (TXN-FLUSH-BEHIND).
- **Records**: new/read-refresh-grid/ (0.25-7 s grid, then the five-update ladder), new/txn-view-refresh-boundary/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-refresh-elapsed/, read-refresh-ladder/.
- **Open**: dependence on PageTimeout (see TXN-VIEW).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-ID: a deleted-and-reinserted row
- **Claim**: After another process deletes row 5 and reinserts the same key and values (twice, the second time with changed values), a held dynaset positioned on it, read after 7 s and Idle, answers 3167 "Record is deleted" for every field at that position (its Bookmark bytes unchanged, 01070000) and neighbours read normally; `Requery` shows the reinserted row (N=51, V=9) at the same place with different bookmark bytes (01030000); a new snapshot shows it.
- **Records**: new/read-id-delete-reinsert-late/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-id-delete-reinsert/ (stale, superseded).
- **Open**: a bookmark can differ because Requery rebuilds the keyset, so bookmark equality is not an identity test.

### R-LOCK-3 READ-SCHEMA: held descriptors across another process's DDL (partial)
- **Claim**: When the changing process's counterpart holds no cursor on the table, another process's `ALTER TABLE ADD COLUMN`, `CREATE INDEX`, `DROP TABLE` and `CREATE TABLE` all succeed; the first process's held TableDef still answers (Name, Fields.Count 3 and Indexes.Count 1) after 7 s and Idle, with no error; a refreshed `TableDefs` Item shows the new 4 fields; the held Fields collection shows 4 after its own `Refresh`; a new `SELECT *` reads the recreated table (with the new column).
- **Records**: new/read-schema-held-descriptors-late/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/read-schema-held-descriptors/ (3262 with held cursors).
- **Open**: the held QueryDef part was never exercised (the changing process's QueryDefs collection was unrefreshed, `Item("QT")` 3265).

### R-LOCK-3 READ-SNAPSHOT: unread rows
- **Claim**: A snapshot opened before another process updates rows 20 and 40 shows their open-time values when first read after 7 s and Idle (N=200, 400, old S) and the new values only after `Requery`; a dynaset opened at the same time shows the new values (7777, 8888, 'changed') at first read of those rows; its first row, read before the update, was not re-read.
- **Records**: new/read-snapshot-unread-rows/ (three rows on separate pages).
- **Open**: lazy materialization of large snapshots; callbacks (T).

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-TIMING: edit ownership vs writers
- **Claim**: A pending pessimistic `Edit` of an unindexed field (V) of row 1 blocks another process's update of an unindexed field (3260) and of an indexed field (N) of row 2 (same page), with the ADO locking mode first-opened as page (0) and as row (1, error 3218 "currently locked"); it admits V of row 64 and an INSERT; after `CancelUpdate` the row 2 update succeeds. Also: optimistic/`dbSeeChanges` admits all; the block lasts only while `Edit` is pending (admitted after CancelUpdate, Update, recordset Close or release, `Database.Close`); `dbDenyWrite` gives 3262 for UPDATE/table open and `dbDenyRead` 3261.
- **Records**: new/write-timing-unindexed-field-page-mode/, -row-mode/ (field and mode variants); evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-timing-pessimistic-matrix/, write-timing-optimistic-seechanges/, write-timing-release-boundaries/, write-timing-ado-lock-mode-page/, -row/, write-deny-options/.
- **Open**: the release probe never showed the Edit still pending at each step.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-ORIGINAL: first Update conflicts, second Update overwrites
- **Claim**: With an optimistic dynaset edit (N = 100) pending, another process changing the same row (N), only another field (V), changes the row so that the first `Update` fails 3197 (`EditMode` stays 1); a second `Update` succeeds (`EditMode` 0) and overwrites the whole row, so the other field's change is lost (final independent read: N=100, V=0); the other process changing N and restoring the original value gives no conflict (first Update succeeds; a second Update gives 3020). Final values read by the other process after a 7 s pause and Idle.
- **Records**: new/write-original-matrix-final-read/ (three rows on separate pages); evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-original-same-row/, write-original-same-row-mdb/, write-original-changed-then-restored/.
- **Open**: a change to another row on the same page.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-ALLOC: an uncommitted insert blocks the page it lands on
- **Claim**: While another process holds an uncommitted insert of key 500 into T, an insert of key 501 into T (3260) and an update of row 64 (the last page) (3260) are refused, an insert into another table, an insert into the AutoNumber table and an update of row 1 (first page) are admitted; after the rollback the T insert succeeds.
- **Records**: new/write-alloc-insert-granularity/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-alloc-insert-txn/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/write-alloc-counter-rollback-readid/.
- **Open**: byte-level page identity (W-04); an earlier insert was admitted while all rows of the table were updated (write-promote), which depends on free space.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-TREE: concurrent uniqueness candidates
- **Claim**: While another process holds an uncommitted insert, inserts of the same primary key, other keys and the same secondary-unique value fail 3260; after its rollback they insert; after its commit the same key or value gives 3022. With WRITE-ALLOC the block is the page the new row lands on.
- **Records**: evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/write-tree-unique-secondary/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-tree-unique-candidate/, write-alloc-insert-txn/.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-LV: memo replacement vs a positioned or editing recordset (partial)
- **Claim**: With another process's dynaset merely positioned on row 1 of a table of 3,000-character memos (no Edit, no memo read), replacing the memo of rows 1 to 4 (3,500 characters) succeeds; with `Edit` pending on row 1, replacing rows 1, 2 and 3 fails 3260; after `CancelUpdate` and Close the replacement succeeds and all lengths read back (3,501, 3,500 x3).
- **Records**: new/write-lv-replace-granularity/; evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-lv-replace-reuse/.
- **Open**: the earlier 3186 (dynaset sitting on row 4 of a long-memo table) is not reproduced; whether reading the memo is what produces it is unasked.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-DDL: schema changes beside other users
- **Claim**: Another process's `ALTER TABLE`, `CREATE INDEX`, `DROP TABLE` and `DROP INDEX` fail 3262 while a process has cursors open, a pending pessimistic Edit (also after CancelUpdate with the recordset open) or an uncommitted DDL; its `SELECT` of a table under uncommitted DDL gives 3261; all succeed after the other side closes or rolls back, and also while the other process holds only descriptors (TableDef, QueryDef).
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-ddl-vs-pending-edit/, read-schema-held-descriptors/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/write-ddl-vs-open-transaction-ddl-first/, cat-xproc-drop-table-under-reader/; new/read-schema-held-descriptors-late/.

### R-LOCK-4 TXN-RANGE: an unrelated admitted change survives the other's resolution
- **Claim**: While process a holds a transaction updating N of row 1, process b updates V of row 64 (far) and U row 1 (admitted) and V of row 2 (same page, 3260); after a's Rollback (phase 1) b reads row 1 N=10, row 64 V=1, U N=11 and a reads the same; after a's CommitTrans (phase 2) both read row 1 N=1010, row 64 V=2, U N=12 (b after 7 s and Idle; a after 7 s and Idle).
- **Records**: new/txn-range-far-row-final-read/; evidence/r-fix-cat-txn/txn-local-xproc-same-page-retry/, local-two-rows-far-b-commit-a-rollback/ (in-process).
- **Open**: lock release at an inner level.

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-PROMOTE: lock budget
- **Claim**: Exceeding `dbMaxLocksPerFile` raises 3035 (statement fails, `RecordsAffected` 0, transaction usable, Rollback works, other process admitted); no promotion is observed: another table and an INSERT stay admitted while a process holds 64 row updates.
- **Records**: evidence/r-lock-3-4-txn/write-promote-bulk-update/; evidence/r-txn-1/exhaust-locks-*/.
- **Open**: the default threshold (no getter).

### R-LOCK-4 WRITE-FAIL: a killed holder
- **Claim**: With another process holding an uncommitted update of row 1 and a pessimistic Edit on row 40, writes to both fail 3260; 1.5 s after that process is killed (TerminateProcess) both writes succeed, the uncommitted N change is gone (row 1 N=10, V incremented by the survivor, independent read after 7 s and Idle), and the lock file stays at 128 B.
- **Records**: new/write-fail-kill-lock-holder/.
- **Open**: the release time inside 1.5 s; injected failures (T).

### R-CRASH-1 CRASH-KILL: process-termination images open cleanly (serves CRASH-CAPTURE)
- **Claim**: In the live-OS process-termination model, each of 45 frozen images (34 kill points on ACCDB and MDB, 12 mid-call kills, one uncommitted bulk transaction) opens ACE-first with `hr=0`, no error, no repair request and an unchanged file; Compact-first also succeeds; uncommitted transactions (also a 2,048-row insert in a transaction killed 1.5 s after Execute: 13.0 MB file, 2,048 rows visible), pending AddNew, edited dynaset rows, implicit statements, LV replace and bulk insert are absent; committed (default and forced) and closed states are present; the lock file stays 64 B after a shared writer is killed and is absent after an exclusive writer.
- **Conditions**: `TerminateProcess`, OS and cache alive, kill at API boundaries or 20/100/400/1500 ms into a call; not power loss, no write recording.
- **Records**: evidence/r-crash-1-kill/* (32 kill records), evidence/r-crash-1-midcall/* (12), new/crash-txn-kill-between-execute-and-commit/.
- **Open**: kill during creation (kills at 5, 40 and 150 ms found no file, creation takes about 250 ms: new/crash-new-kill-mid-create-*; 200-300 ms untested), Access-first, CalcDb cuts, power loss (T).

## R-CURSOR-1, R-DAO-1 and R-ERR-1

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-POS: position, BOF/EOF and Move transitions are one rule set across table, dynaset and snapshot; forward-only is a restricted subset

- **Claim.** On table-type, dynaset and snapshot: `MovePrevious` on the first row gives BOF without error, a second gives 3021; `MoveNext` past the last row gives EOF, a second gives 3021; `Move 0` at BOF/EOF gives 3021; `Move n` and `Move -n` past either end give EOF/BOF without error; `Move -1` from EOF and `Move 1` from BOF reach the last/first row. `AbsolutePosition` is -1 at BOF/EOF and 0-based otherwise (table-type: 3251). An empty table has BOF and EOF true and every move gives 3021. Deleting the current row through the recordset itself leaves BOF/EOF false and the next field read fails 3021 on a dynaset (3167 on table-type); a row deleted by another cursor reads 3167 on a dynaset. A failed `FindNext`/`FindPrevious` sets NoMatch and leaves the position; a later `Move` resets NoMatch. Forward-only: `MovePrevious`, `MoveFirst`, `MoveLast`, negative `Move` and `AbsolutePosition` fail 3219; `Move 0` and positive moves work. `Move` takes fractional and digit-string counts rounded to a long (2.5 and 1.5 both move 2), refuses Null and text with 3421, and accepts the 32-bit limits.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, all four types; the table-type and dynaset/snapshot move set also on an MDB (Jet 4). Find family on dynaset and snapshot only (table-type 3251, forward-only 3219).
- **Records.** c1/pos-moves-dynaset, pos-moves-snapshot, pos-moves-table, pos-moves-forwardonly, pos-empty-singleton, pos-delete-ends, pos-find; c1b/move-argument-forms, find-duplicates-sequence, find-all-directions-other-types, find-empty-and-ends; def/mdb-pos-table, mdb-pos-dynaset, mdb-pos-snapshot, delete-by-other-cursor-current-row; fix/clone-write-visibility-same-row. Discriminating: delete by the cursor itself (3021) versus by another cursor (3167): c1/pos-delete-ends against def/delete-by-other-cursor-current-row and fix/clone-write-visibility-same-row. q/id-held-field-after-delete-reinsert repeats the 3167 reading of a row deleted by another call.
- **Open.** `Move(2147483647)` on forward-only gives 3219 while `Move 10` works; forward-only `RecordCount` is -1 on a singleton but grows on a larger table; Find with quote and Like criteria differs on an indexed Text column (fix/find-text-quotes-unindexed, find-like-patterns-unindexed: four criteria match on FU, NoMatch on FI). Lanes other than raw IDispatch.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-EMPTY-PAGES (public part): a table whose rows are all deleted, and a table with deleted ranges, behave as empty or as the surviving rows on every type

- **Claim.** A table whose 256 rows were all deleted reports BOF and EOF true, `RecordCount` 0 and every move 3021 on table, table with `Index`, dynaset, snapshot and forward-only (forward-only 3219 for `MoveFirst`, `MoveLast`, `MovePrevious`). A table with deleted id ranges returns the survivors in storage order (table-type without Index) or key order (with Index, and dynasets over the table or an ORDER BY), both directions, EOF true at the end of the forward walk and BOF at the end of the backward walk; table-type `RecordCount` is 228 at open.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; fixtures MA (256 rows deleted) and M2 (shuffled storage order, 228 survivors). Whether any page is actually empty is not established.
- **Records.** c1/pages-all-deleted, pages-traverse-table, pages-traverse-dynaset; fix/create-pages, pages-traverse-storage-order (discriminating: storage and key order differ).
- **Open.** Page-level emptiness, overflow-only pages, budget and I/O failures (T: W-04 bytes, fault injection).

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-POP: RecordCount, AbsolutePosition and PercentPosition by type; the dynaset frontier; ORDER BY snapshots are complete at open

- **Claim.** Table-type reports the exact `RecordCount` at open. A dynaset reports 1 at open and after a field read; the first `MoveNext` populates up to 500 further rows (228-row table: the full 228; 2048-row `SELECT ... ORDER BY` source: 501); moves inside that range leave the count (positions 10, 100, 500: 501); a jump beyond it populates exactly to the row reached (position 2000: 2001); `MoveLast` gives 2048, and `MoveLast` alone on a new recordset also gives 2048. `Requery` returns a dynaset to 1. A snapshot over a plain table reports 1 at open and the full count after the first move (c1, def); a snapshot over `SELECT ... ORDER BY` on the 2048-row table reports 2048 at open and after every move, and its values are fixed at open: UPDATE of rows 2, 1000 and 2000 issued after open, after the first move, or after `MoveLast`+`MoveFirst` is never seen (all reads before and after identical, `V` not 'chg'). Query sources: DISTINCT, TOP, crosstab give 1 at open and the exact count after `MoveLast`; GROUP BY, UNION, LEFT JOIN give the exact count at open. Forward-only counts the rows fetched. `AbsolutePosition` is `RecordCount`-1 at the last row and `PercentPosition` is 100*Abs/Count on dynaset/snapshot (a different scale on table-type); `PercentPosition` is assignable on a table recordset and on dynaset/snapshot only after `MoveLast`.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; MDB rows for the 8-row table and query sources; sizes 8, 228, 512 and 2048 rows; the 2048-row cases through an ORDER BY query source.
- **Records.** q/pop-frontier-dynaset-big (discriminating: minimal observation `MoveLast` alone equals the instrumented), pop-frontier-snapshot-big, pop-snapshot-change-after-open, pop-snapshot-change-after-first-move, pop-snapshot-change-after-movelast; c1/pop-count-dynaset, pop-count-snapshot, pop-count-table, pop-count-forwardonly, pop-count-minimal-dynaset, pop-visibility-snapshot; def/mdb-pos-dynaset, mdb-pos-snapshot, pos-query-*; fix/pages-traverse-storage-order; c1b/percent-assign.
- **Open (F).** Whether ORDER BY or table size makes a snapshot materialise at open, and whether a large PLAIN-table snapshot still fixes values lazily per row as c1/pop-visibility-snapshot showed on 5 rows: one probe each (snapshot of the plain 2048-row table; snapshot of an ORDER BY on the 8-row table). Other Workspace/process controls (T: R-LOCK-3/4).

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-ID: Bookmark representation, assignment errors and the held Field across delete and reinsert

- **Claim.** `Bookmark` is `VT_ARRAY|VT_UI1`, 4 bytes, lower bound 0; forward-only is not bookmarkable (3219). Dynaset: a 16-bit little-endian number in bytes 0-1 (row 1 = 0x0200, rows 2-255 +1 each, row 256 = 0x02ff, row 257 = 0x0300); it is NOT row ordinal + 0x200 beyond the first few hundred rows (row 1000 = 0x065d, row 2048 = 0x0aeb), and byte 2-3 are 0. Snapshot: the same shape with base 0x0300 (`02030000` at row 3). Table-type (with or without `Index`, identical under different indexes): byte 0 is the slot within a data page and byte 1 the page (row 1 of Big = `00 3e`, row 255 = `02 4c`, 256 = `03 4c`, 1000 = `09 77`, last = `0d b5`; small table pages `2d`). Revisiting a row returns equal bytes; the bytes are unchanged by a key change and by growing a row to 255 characters. After an external DELETE of the current row, assigning the earlier-read bookmark of that row succeeds, keeps BOF/EOF false and `AbsolutePosition`, and field reads give 3167; a bookmark for an ordinal past the end gives 3159, after which there is no current row (field read 3021); on a snapshot assigning the row's own bookmark succeeds. Empty array and `""` succeed and move nothing; four zero bytes give 3159 and no current row; Long, Null and Empty give 3421 and keep the position; a bookmark of another recordset type gives 3159. A held recordset Field after DELETE of its row reads 3167, still 3167 after reinsertion of the same key (the new row is not shown), after `Move 0`, and when a walk reaches that slot; after `Requery` it reads the first row. `LastModified` is null on a fresh recordset and equals `Bookmark` after Update. `Index` switch keeps the current row as the first row of the new index.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; bookmarks assigned as constants (a captured SAFEARRAY cannot be passed back).
- **Records.** q/id-bookmark-bytes-by-row (discriminating: ordinals 255/256/257/1000/2048 on three cursors), id-bookmark-assign-deleted-and-beyond, id-held-field-after-delete-reinsert; c1/id-bookmark-shape, id-key-change, id-relocation, id-delete-reinsert, id-index-switch, id-field-held; fix/id-bookmark-assign-fresh (each assignment on a fresh dynaset); c1b/bookmark-walk-types, clone-bookmark-sharing, lastmodified-addnew.
- **Open (F).** Validity of a retained bookmark after `Requery`: q/id-bookmark-after-requery-and-reinsert gave 3159 for the earlier-read row, but after `Requery` the dynaset holds 1 row (`RecordCount` 1), so the result is confounded by population; one probe that populates (MoveLast) after `Requery` before assigning decides it. Mapping of the dynaset number beyond row 255 (the extra 118 at row 1000 and 236 at row 2048). (T) Relocation confirmation, split, allocation reuse, file-copy restore (bytes).

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-SEEK: operators follow index order; short and omitted keys are prefix bounds; duplicates select the first; long keys do not collide up to 255 characters

- **Claim.** On a table-type recordset with `Index` set, `=`, `>=`, `<`, `<=` and `>` select by index order, not value order (a descending component swaps `<` and `>`). With duplicates `=`, `>=` and `<=` select the first duplicate and `>`/`<` the row after/before the group, also across leaves. Short keys (fewer values than index columns) act as a prefix: `=`, `>=` and `>` all land on the FIRST row whose prefix is at or after the key (so `>` behaves like `>=`), and `<` and `<=` both land on the last row before that group, or NoMatch with no current row if none precedes; identical from a first-row and a last-row start. On IX_ABC (A asc, B desc, C asc): key (1): `=`,`>=`,`>` give (1,3,1), `<`,`<=` NoMatch; (2): `=`,`>=`,`>` give (2,3,1), `<`,`<=` give (1,1,2); (1,2): (1,2,1) and (1,3,2); (2,1): (2,1,1) and (2,2,2); full key (1,2,1): `=`,`>=`,`<=` (1,2,1), `>` (1,2,2), `<` (1,3,2). A `Missing` component truncates the key at that position (leading Missing = empty key = first row, middle = prefix before it); `Null` and `Empty` are real key values: `=` with one gives NoMatch (no current row), `>=` lands at the position of Null (lowest in an ascending component, last in a descending one: (1,Null,1) `>=` gives (2,3,1), (1,2,Null) `>=` gives (1,2,1)). Text keys of 100, 250, 254 and 255 characters that differ only near the end are distinct entries: each full key finds its own row, `>`/`<`/`>=` follow plain string order; a 250-character prefix `=` gives NoMatch and `>=` the first longer key. A text index ignores case and a trailing blank (`abc`, `ABC`, `"abc "` land together); `=""`, `="*"` and a Long key give NoMatch. A Date key must carry its time part. Refusals: `<>` and every other operator spelling give 3001 (Null operator 3421), no key 3001, more keys than columns 3250 (13 keys; 14 give 3001), non-numeric text for a Long key 3421, no `Index` 3019, dynaset 3251. A refusal leaves the start row current. `Null` sorts first (`IX_K`: `=,Null` lands on the Null row; an IGNORE NULL index gives NoMatch).
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; IX_ABC fixture of 13 rows, start rows chosen so no landing equals a start, plus the two-start repair fixtures.
- **Records.** q/seek-short-keys-from-first, seek-short-keys-from-last (discriminating: identical landings from two starts), seek-omission-positions, seek-long-key-collision; c1/seek-operators, seek-duplicates, seek-duplicates-multileaf; fix/seek-arguments-from-two-starts, seek-duplicates-null-from-last, seek-operator-spelling-controls; c1b/seek-gap-keys, seek-text-index, seek-date-index, seek-repeat-sequence; fix/index-metadata-seek-pk-name. Reconciles c1b/seek-three-column-mixed (partial `>` like `>=`; NoMatch exactly when no row precedes the group).
- **Open.** Linked/ODBC and replication cases, binary and other index types, text longer than 255 (T).

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-EDIT: edit buffer lifecycle, validation order, AutoNumber reservation, FK and Required-Memo edits

- **Claim.** `Edit` gives EditMode 1, `AddNew` 2 (generated AutoNumber read at AddNew, defaults shown, other fields Null), `Update` and `CancelUpdate` 0; a second `Edit` reverts the buffer to the stored value; a second `Update` or `CancelUpdate` gives 3020. `Move`, `Requery`, `FindFirst`, `GetRows`, a Bookmark assignment and `Close` discard a pending edit; `Clone`, `Filter`, `Sort`, `OpenRecordset`, `RecordCount` and external writes keep it. A violation at `Update` leaves EditMode and the buffer; check order: Required/NOT NULL (3314) before the field rule (3316) before the unique index (3022); the table rule gives 3316; assignment order does not change it. `ValidateOnSet True` raises 3316 at the assignment, EditMode stays 1. Relationship violations at `Update` leave EditMode and buffer: a child with a missing parent 3201 (AddNew EditMode 2, Edit EditMode 1), a parent key change or Delete with children 3200 (Delete leaves the current row and EditMode 0); the stored tables are unchanged. A Required Memo made with DDL `NOT NULL`: `Update` with the Memo unset gives 3314 (EditMode 2) and again with Null, a zero-length string is accepted and stored (a Required zero-length Text gives 3315, fix/con-zero-length-required-failonerror). AutoNumber: the value is reserved at `AddNew` and is never reused: `AddNew`+`CancelUpdate` (1), `AddNew`+`Update` inside a transaction then `Rollback` (2), a new `AddNew`+`CancelUpdate` (3) and, after the Database is closed and reopened, the next `AddNew` gives 4, and only that row is stored. After `Update` the current row stays the old row; the new row's `LastModified` equals its `Bookmark`. After `Rollback` of the Workspace the recordset reads 3420 and `LastModified` is a null array. A successful `Update` leaves the previous `Errors` entry. Snapshot and forward-only refuse Edit/AddNew/Update/Delete 3251 and field set 3027.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB; table-type and dynaset.
- **Records.** q/edit-addnew-counter-rollback-reopen (discriminating: cancel, rollback, reopen in one sequence), edit-fk-violation, edit-required-memo-addnew; c1/edit-addnew-generated, edit-buffer-lifecycle, edit-move-close-buffered, edit-validation-order, edit-cancel-rollback; fix/edit-validation-errors-per-failure (paired failures and both assignment orders); c1b/edit-pending-ops, editmode-by-type, editmode-unusual-transitions, update-arguments; def/assign-without-edit-by-type, field-value-trio-during-edit; fix/assign-without-edit-own-transaction.
- **Open.** Time/random/UDF defaults (R-EXPR-1/R-UDF-1). Probe defect: q/edit-conflict-other-row-and-restore and q/edit-required-memo-addnew call `CancelUpdate`/set after a successful `Update`, leaving a harmless 3020.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-EDIT (conflicts): an optimistic Update is refused 3197 when the stored row image differs from the one read; a pessimistic edit locks other Workspaces

- **Claim.** Optimistic: A's first `Update` gives 3197 when B changed the same field or another field of the row (EditMode stays 1, buffer kept); a second explicit `Update` then succeeds and overwrites B's change (A's value stored, B's lost, read through both Databases). `Update` succeeds when B (second Database of the same Workspace) changed another ROW of the table (ID 3 while A edits ID 2), changed another field of the same row and restored it (S 's4' to 'tmp' and back while A edits N), ran `UPDATE T SET N = N` on the same row, or changed and restored the same field (def). So the conflict test compares the row image, not a modification counter: two real writes that leave the image unchanged do not conflict. `dbSeeChanges` (512) makes `Edit` itself give 3197 when the row changed after the recordset was read. Pessimistic: B (second Workspace) `Edit` on A's locked row gives 3188 (also another row of the same table); after A's `Update` B edits and updates.
- **Conditions.** Two Databases of one Workspace (optimistic); second appended Workspace (pessimistic); dynaset; ACCDB; rows of the 8-row table share one page (not verified).
- **Records.** q/edit-conflict-other-row-and-restore (discriminating: (c) other row, (f2) restore of another field, (f3) no-op update); fix/edit-conflict-optimistic-readback, edit-conflict-pessimistic-second-workspace; def/edit-conflict-restore-value, field-value-trio-other-writer; c1b/option-seechanges; e1b/lock-optimistic-conflict, lock-pessimistic-delete.
- **Open.** (T) (d) body-only relocation and (e) delete/reinsert incarnation need native bytes to confirm the condition; whether the "other row" result also holds on a different page.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-PROV: updatability and provenance by source shape; edits reach the current row; Missing Type cell changes option 4

- **Claim.** Dynaset `Updatable` is True for a plain table, DISTINCTROW, TOP and inner/LEFT joins; False for DISTINCT, GROUP BY, UNION and TRANSFORM (edit 3027, Update 3020, crosstab `Requery` 3251); every snapshot is not updatable. `DataUpdatable` is True for base fields and aliases, False for expressions and aggregates (`SourceTable` empty for aggregates; an expression keeps the first referenced column as `SourceField`). Two aliases of one source field update together. In a join a parent-field edit through one child row shows in the other child row and cursor; a child-only edit changes only that child; the join key can be set or refused (3101 missing parent, 3164 parent key). On the null-extended row of a LEFT JOIN a child edit fails 3058 (EditMode stays 1) and a parent edit succeeds; `AddNew` with only the absent side fails 3101. `AddNew` with only parent fields creates a parent row, with only child fields 3164 (omitted/consistent); under option 16 child fields are assignable and referential integrity still refuses (3200, 3201, 3022); `Delete` removes the child row only or fails 3200 (option 16). Two equal heap rows, and a projection WITHOUT the key over two equal rows: an `Edit`/`Update` or `Delete` with the cursor on the SECOND of the two changes that second row (not the first) on dynaset and on table-type; the identity is the current row, not a lookup by value. Options: a writable open gives `Updatable` 0 for option 4 on table-type and dynaset when Type is given (`("T",1,4)`, `("T",1,4,Missing)`, `("T",2,4)`: Edit gives 3027 "Cannot update. Database or object is read-only."); with the Type cell `Missing` (`("T",Missing,4)`) the result is a writable table-type (Type 1, `Updatable` True, Edit works); `("T",Missing,4,3)` gives 3001; option 8 gives RecordCount 0; 256 not bookmarkable; 16|32 gives 3001.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, writable opens (the read-only opens of def/prov-* field metadata are void and repeated in fix).
- **Records.** q/prov-heap-second-row-edit, prov-missing-key-second-row-edit (discriminating: cursor moved to the second equal row, which def/prov-heap-duplicate-rows and prov-missing-projected-key never did), prov-readonly-option-forms (discriminating: the same call with the Type cell given and Missing; explains fix/open-argument-shapes-rw against option-flags-rw); fix/pos-query-sources-rw, field-metadata-rw, prov-sources-errors-per-call, prov-outer-join-cancel-after-failed-update, option-flags-rw, option-join-addnew-delete-omitted/inconsistent/consistent, updatable-members-rw; def/prov-alias-edit-duplicate-source, prov-parent-edit-two-children, prov-join-field-change, prov-inconsistent-referential-integrity; c1b/option-join-updatability, option-append-only.
- **Open.** Why a Missing Type cell drops the read-only effect of option 4 (observed, not explained; an ACE quirk the adapter must reproduce). Linked-source identity (outside the local scope of this card).

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-BULK: GetRows array shape, counts, short return, deleted rows ahead, edit interplay

- **Claim.** `GetRows` returns a `VT_ARRAY|VT_VARIANT` `[0+fields, 0+rows]` with the stored types' cell tags (Long I4, Yes/No BOOL, Byte UI1, Integer I2, Currency CY, Double R8, Single R4, Date DATE, Text/Memo BSTR with the whole Memo, binary `VT_ARRAY|VT_UI1`, Null `VT_NULL`, all-Null row `VT_BOOL 0` for Yes/No and `VT_NULL` elsewhere), identical on all four types. A Decimal(18,4) made by ADO DDL reads as DECIMAL scale 4. There is no row ceiling: `GetRows(1500)` returns 1500 rows on a dynaset and a snapshot (2048-row source), the next `GetRows(1000)` returns the remaining 548 with EOF true, and one more at EOF gives 3021. Count arguments: 0, -1 and True give 3001; digit strings and fractions convert (2.5 gives 2); text and Null 3421; a Missing count or no argument returns 1; on an empty table or at EOF 3021. During `Edit`/`AddNew` `GetRows` moves and discards the edit (EditMode 0, then `CancelUpdate` 3020). Rows deleted by another call AHEAD of the cursor are skipped by `GetRows` and by `MoveNext` alike (rows 5 and 6 deleted: `GetRows(4)` from ID 1 returns IDs 1-4 and leaves the cursor on ID 7, `AbsolutePosition` 4; `MoveNext` over a deleted ID 2 lands on ID 3). Local `CacheSize` accepts 0, -1, 1201 and `"10"` and reads 0; `FillCache` and `CacheStart` accept every argument; table-type `Cache*` give 3251 and `FillCache` 3219.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, local.
- **Records.** q/bulk-getrows-large-count, bulk-getrows-deletion-discovery; c1b/getrows-count-arguments, getrows-beyond-eof, getrows-shape-types, getrows-deleted-row, getrows-edit-buffer, cache-size-properties, cache-fill-and-start; def/bulk-getrows-memo-binary; fix/getrows-decimal-ado-ddl.
- **Open (F).** `GetRows` when the cursor is ON a row that another call just deleted (the q probe's `MoveNext` skipped the deleted row, so the "discovered before GetRows" state was never reached; it needs the deletion after the cursor sits on the row). (T) Large-value resource failures, ODBC CacheSize.

### R-CURSOR-1 CUR-LIFE: Clone, Filter/Sort, Requery and Close relations of a recordset family; shared population

- **Claim.** A clone of a table, dynaset or snapshot starts on the first row with its own position and bookmark; the parent's moves do not move it; forward-only cannot be cloned (3219); `Clone(4)`, `Clone(2)` and Null give 3001. A dynaset bookmark is accepted by its clone and a separate dynaset (same row) and survives the parent's `Close`; a bookmark of another recordset type gives 3159. A clone does not inherit Filter or Sort. Population is shared by the family: a clone made after the parent's `MoveLast` reports `RecordCount` 2048 at once (`AbsolutePosition` 0); a clone made before population reports 1 as does the parent, and after either `MoveLast` both report 2048. A clone's `Update` sets its `LastModified`, not the parent's, and the reverse; a clone's edit is seen in the parent at once on the same row, and a clone's Delete reads 3167 in the parent. A clone and a derived recordset survive the parent's `Close`; a closed object gives 3420 for every member including `Close`. `Filter`/`Sort` change nothing in the recordset; `OpenRecordset()` derives a dynaset (snapshot from a snapshot), always `Updatable` False, with errors at the derived open. `Requery` on dynaset and snapshot returns to row 1 with `RecordCount` 1; held clones and children then read 3021 until a move; table-type `Requery`, `Filter` and `Sort` give 3251. Closing a clone that was created INSIDE the explicit transaction has no effect on the transaction or the work: with a clone closed after Update then `Rollback` the row is back, with a pending Edit it stays EditMode 1 and a later Update and `CommitTrans` store it, after Update then `CommitTrans` it is stored, nested (inner Rollback, inner Commit then outer Rollback) behave as ordinary transactions, and with no transaction the stored row is unchanged.
- **Conditions.** ACCDB, dynaset/snapshot/table; clone-in-transaction cases on a dynaset of T.
- **Records.** q/life-clone-population-order, life-clone-close-transaction-one-level, life-clone-close-transaction-nested (discriminating: cases 1-3 and the nested ones for clones created inside the transaction); c1/life-clone, life-clone-close-transaction, life-filter-sort, life-requery, life-close-held; c1b/clone-by-type-and-properties, clone-chain-close-orders, clone-bookmark-sharing, lastmodified-clone-family, filter-*, sort-expression-forms, requery-types-state, requery-family; fix/clone-write-visibility-same-row; def/recordset-closed-member-inventory.
- **Open (F).** Observed once, not qualified: a clone created BEFORE `BeginTrans` and closed inside the transaction (after an Update) failed 3246 "Operation not supported in transactions", the update was not stored and the following `Rollback` gave 3034 (the transaction had ended) in q/life-clone-close-transaction-nested case 3; needs repeats with Commit, no Update, and two levels. (T) Another Database enlisted (R-TXN-1).

### R-DAO-1 DAO-BOOT: first touch creates the default Workspace; SystemDB is empty only before it

- **Claim.** The first touch of the default session by `Workspaces`, `OpenDatabase` or `BeginTrans` each creates one workspace "#Default Workspace#", user "admin", type 2. `DBEngine.SystemDB` reads "" before the session exists and "system.mdb" after any touch; `Properties("SystemDB").Value` read as the first member gives "system.mdb" and the direct getter then agrees (so the d1b difference between the direct getter and the Properties item was read order). `DefaultUser("Admin")` set before first use gives `UserName` "Admin". After `Close` of the default Workspace `Count` is 1, the wrapper and `Name` still read, `OpenDatabase` still works, and the earlier Database gives 3420. `CreateWorkspace` does not append (`Count` unchanged) and the unappended workspace opens a database; a duplicate name appends and `Item(name)` returns the first; an empty name gives 3271. The same file opened twice in one Workspace gives two Database objects (`Count` 2, `Item(name)` the first), a third open exclusive succeeds. `Rollback`/`CommitTrans` without `BeginTrans` give 3034. `IniPath` reads "" and `DefaultType` 2.
- **Conditions.** One process per probe (first touch isolated); ACCDB/MDB.
